push-to-deploy runs the build command set in the Cloudflare dashboard and not the one in
`wrangler.toml`. Un-committing `webapp/` before that setting exists would publish an empty site on
the next push. B24 is that step.

**One factual error, caught by the subagent that wrote the page, not by me.** My first `platform.md`
said of Mission Control and the phone HUD: "none of it has run a full game in the field." That is
false and the handoff two directories away says so: a 300 s FFA on 2026-08-30 (two iPhones, MacBook
host) and an outdoor TDM on 2026-09-01 (two Android HUDs). I had told the agent not to overclaim, and
it complied and then flagged that complying had made the page harsher than the evidence. The page now
has a "What runs, but is not finished" section that says what ran and when. The counting limits it
also flagged as lost (63 players, 4 native teams, 1 gun per node, 3 guns proven on one radio) are back
too, as a table, because they are bench facts rather than positioning. **The lesson is the cheap half
of measurement discipline: "don't overclaim" and "state what happened" are different instructions,
and an instruction to be conservative can produce a false statement just as an instruction to sell
can.**

**The push broke the site, and the gate said 20/20.** The 74-to-9 cut orphaned 65 URLs, so I added
`_redirects` splat rules to absorb them. `/manual/hardware/*` also matches `/manual/hardware/`, so
each rule redirected its own destination back to itself: seven of the nine live pages sat in an
infinite loop (301 to the no-slash form, 307 back to the slash form, curl gave up at 50 hops). It was
live for about four minutes. **The guard could not see the fault it was built for, which is F40's
shape for the fifth time.** The coverage check asked "is every old URL matched by some rule", and a
self-swallowing splat answers that "yes". It never asked whether a rule matches a page that still
exists. It does now, plus a chained-redirect check, and both were proven by restoring the exact
broken file: the guard names all seven looping pages. Rules are now 65 explicit lines, no splats.
**The deeper miss is that `site/serve.mjs` does not implement `_redirects` at all**, so no amount of
local care could have caught this. The only instrument that could was the deployed site, and I had
not looked at it. Verified against production this time.

**The review team found a published falsehood I had already "fixed" once.** Earlier in the night I
restored the weapons table's fire sound, cycle and heat from the captured `$WEAP` frame and called it
done. The content reviewer pointed out the base stats were the wrong dataset entirely: the table was
rendering `weapons.json`'s top-level `dmg`/`rof`/`rng`, which are **Open BRX's rebalanced 0-100 UI
bars**, under headings reading "Damage", "RoF" and "Range", on a page titled *The complete Callsign
arsenal*. The Assault Rifle published as Damage 8 where its own frame says 9. It also listed 22 rows
including the three Open BRX sidearms, which carry a **copied** Bolt Rifle frame. Everything now comes
off each weapon's own captured frame and only `captured: true` ships: 19 rows, all 19 matching the old
published table. **I had verified the three columns I touched and never questioned the eight I did
not.** That is the same failure as checking the result without checking the control.

**Four more guards that could not see their own fault, all proved by breaking them.** The block-marker
check was `^`-anchored, so `- [download] ...` mid-line shipped a raw DSL marker with the suite green.
The twin check asserted "file exists and has an H1", so rewriting every twin to "# Wrong page" passed;
it now compares bytes. The redirect check read the FILE and never made a request, which is exactly how
the loop reached production; `serve.mjs` now implements `_redirects` and a step drives all 74 frozen
URLs over HTTP. And the build wrote every page and THEN exited 1, so a red build left the bad page in
`webapp/` for the next commit; it validates before the first write now. **That is F40 five times in one
session, and the pattern in every case is a guard that reads the artefact instead of exercising the
behaviour.**

**Suites:** 21/21 site Playwright steps, 1,059 mcp tests (8 failures are the brx-led session's
in-flight `presentation.py`/`poolgauge.py` edits, not this work: they pass with those files at HEAD),
14 docs-hygiene and published-build tests. Exit codes read directly.

### 2026-09-09 (bench, evening) — 🔴 RETRACTED: an empty `$GLED` colour token is RED, not "keep"; and an emptied pool was lying to the player

Tony on the gun, walking the A16.3 bar state by state. Two real faults, one of them a protocol belief we had
promoted into the public manual that morning.

**1. ⚠️ RETRACTION — an EMPTY colour token parses as 0, which is RED.** It does NOT keep that LED's colour. The
belief dates from 2026-09-07 and A16.3's partial-level blink was built on it: the blink half named only the
segment being turned off (`$GLED,,9,,0,10`) and left the other two blank to "keep" them. On the gun that painted
**red · dark · red**. Tony, watching an armour bar: *"it started animating red purple red"*. Controlled test, one
variable, strip held at three solid purple, that exact frame → red · dark · red. Blink frames now write all three
colours explicitly (`_blink_frame` is just `segment_frame` one segment shorter); it costs a few bytes and removes
a dependency on firmware behaviour we plainly did not understand.

**Why it reached hardware, which is the part worth keeping.** The stage's LED SIMULATOR — built the same evening
precisely so this class of thing could be seen before it reached a gun — implemented the SAME wrong rule, so it
drew the blink cleanly. Tony: *"stage did not show that"*. A simulator that encodes an assumption instead of a
measurement does not predict the gun, **it launders the assumption**: it reproduced my own belief back at me with
the authority of a picture. No test could have caught it either, because every test compared our frames against
our own reading of the firmware. `test_led_invariants` now forbids the SHAPE — no compiled `$GLED` may leave a
colour token empty under an applying gate, the blank (gate 5, colours not read) exempted — and that guard was
proven to fail on the exact frame that shipped.

**2. An emptied pool painted darkness and held it, while the player was at FULL HEALTH.** A shot took armour
35 → 0 with health untouched at 45/45. The bar drained purple to level 0, which is an all-dark frame, and sat
dark for the whole 4 s hold before reverting. Tony: *"it went dark dark dark and then reset to dim blue"*, then
the question that named the bug — *"was it 0 armor and full hp?"* It was. **The strip read "nothing left" at 100%
health**: not an unhelpful display, a FALSE one, at the moment a player most needs the truth.

Fixed as **A16.5**: the drain still animates all the way to zero (losing the plating is not hidden), then after
one `step_ms` beat the readout hands over INWARD to the next pool with anything in it — shield → armour → health.
Armour breaking now reads "plating gone, here is what is left of you", which needs no explanation. Health
emptying hands over to nothing: that is death, and death is deliberately hands-off. One shared rule
(`poolgauge.handover_pool`) with both consumers built against it rather than against each other.

**3. The first cut of the handover was DEAD ON THE GUN and the suite was green.** `pg.handover_pool` where
`stage.py` imports poolgauge as `_pg` — a `NameError` on every hit, the whole feature doing nothing, 45 stage
tests still passing, because the handover branch had **no test at all**. It was visible only because background
tasks log their exceptions, which is a fix from the 2026-09-07 polish round. Without that one line it would have
failed silently and been reported as built. Then the test written to pin it FAILED against working code, because
it matched the first `L0` in the frame list — which is the all-off blink every drop opens with, not the end of the
drain. Two lessons, same shape: **a green suite proves nothing about a branch it never enters**, and a failing
assertion is as likely to be wrong as the code it accuses.

**Also confirmed on the gun tonight:** the dim team rest (A16.4) reads clearly against a full-brightness readout —
purple over dim blue is *"way brighter, reads as an event"*, which is the F56 fix working; the full drop animation
*"animated down showed for a good amount of time then it went back to team color"*; and the corrected blink is
*"purple solid, purple blink, dark"*.

**Still open from this sitting:** for a SINGLE-HUE pool the seven levels are only four visual states — armour's
L1/L2 are both `4,9,9`, L3/L4 both `4,4,9`, L5/L6 both `4,4,4`, differing only by whether the top segment blinks.
Health escapes it because its colour shifts green → yellow → red. So "seven levels" is a promise the hardware
keeps for health alone, and Tony's brief (looped blinking reserved for critical) would collapse armour and shield
to a 4-state bar. Not yet decided.

### 2026-09-09 (bench, late) — the A16 LED language walked state by state on a real gun, and it holds

Tony driving, every call by eye, one state at a time. This is the first end-to-end hardware pass over A16/
A16.3/A16.4/A16.5 and the first time most of it has been seen outside the simulator.

**PASSED, all observed on the gun:**
- **Dim team rest** (A16.4). Pregame full, then 2.5 s after `$SPAWN` the body settles to team colour at
  brightness 1. Verified on the stage AND the gun.
- **The readout contrast, which is the F56 fix.** Full-brightness armour purple over the dim blue rest:
  *"way brighter, reads as an event"*. This is the whole argument for the dim rest and it survives contact.
- **The seven-level ladder, DOWN and back UP.** 45 → 1 → 44 in single-level steps with a few seconds between:
  `grn grn grn` → `grn grn ·` → `yel yel ·` → `yel · ·` → `RED · ·`, and the same in reverse with the hue
  cooling as it climbs. Both directions animate one level per step. Tony: *"it does step up"*.
- **A gain has NO lead freeze and NO all-off blink** — Tony checked for it specifically. By design: a heal
  must not look like a hit. Open question left in FOLLOWUPS on whether it wants an opening beat of its own.
- **Rapid retrigger holds the photosensitivity ceiling.** Five hits landed in **290 ms** (`$HP` 21, 17, 13, 9,
  5) and the strip wrote three level frames with **zero lead freezes and zero all-off blinks between them**;
  two of the five wrote nothing at all because they crossed no level. Exactly the `min_gap_ms` rule working.
- **Critical.** Red appears only at L1, and L1's blink partner is level 0, so critical health pulses red
  against full dark — the loudest thing the strip can do, and it means blink+red says one thing only.
- **Death is hands-off.** `$HP,0,0,0`, then nothing written for 2.5 s, then `$HLOOP,2,750`. No `$GLED` at death.
- **Night mode.** Every gun frame at brightness 1, readout included; armour still read clearly as purple over
  the blue rest. Tony: *"its good"*.
- **The event burst against the new rest.** Three orange flashes, ~140 ms each, alternating to the dim rest and
  ending on it: 3 light-ups in 0.56 s. It reads BETTER than it did against the old dark rest — a bright pulse
  over a live gun rather than a light coming on in a dark room.

**FOUND AND FILED (details in FOLLOWUPS):** F57 the low-health line and the pain grunt firing in the same
millisecond, once per life, at the moment the warning is the point. F58 healing having no configured feedback
at all (`healed` ships `sound=None, gun_led=None`) and `stage.py` never firing it, so the heal path was never
rehearsable on the bench — the eighth stage-vs-phone divergence this week and the first where the stage is
missing a feature rather than mis-copying one. F59 audio lagging the LED by about a second, where the
measurement **killed the obvious theory**: `$PLAY` is enqueued BEFORE the first `$GLED`, so it is not our
ordering; the gun renders light immediately and starts audio late.

**Two false alarms, both resolved rather than left hanging, because filing a wrong finding costs more than
finding nothing.** (1) The gain "snapped" instead of stepping — it did not; that run had both `$HP` frames on
the SAME timestamp (heal readback plus the shot used to force it), so the second change cancelled and
retargeted, which is the correct rule. Driven with a gap it steps properly. (2) `$LIFE` "does not heal" — it
was **refused twice as not on the known-safe list**, and I read the silence as the gun ignoring it. `raw` needs
`confirm=true`. Nearly filed as a defect, which would have undermined S14, whose whole design is MC composing
`$LIFE`. Check that the frame was SENT before concluding anything about how the gun answered.

⚠ **Left genuinely unverified: everything shield-shaped.** The pool is IR-only (P16) and **no compiled mode
registers a medic word** — checked all five, `medic` is `registers: False` in every one, so the `$SIR` table
has no proto-1 row and the gun discards the word silently (the F40 shape). `fn 10` is a known heal and
`_SIR_GRANT` is fns 9-22, so the row is buildable; nothing ships one. The teal shield bar and the shield →
armour handover have never been on a gun and could not be tonight.

⚠ **Also seen and worth knowing at the bench:** `spawn` alone does not reset the GUN's pools, only the stage's
model — the two then disagree silently (model said 45/70 while the gun reported 34/0). `revive`, or a fresh
arm+spawn, is what actually resets a player. And after a gun power-cycle the stage's connection manager keeps a
stale alias that `disconnect` does not clear; restarting the stage is the reliable reset.

**Both open LED design questions DECIDED the same evening, on the gun** (recorded in `led-language.md` §3.1
so a later pass cannot re-propose them): a partial level **keeps** its settled loop-blink — *"the loop
blinking is ok in between stages"* — and **healing gets no opening beat** — *"healing is fine as is"*.

The blink decision also retires the "seven levels are only four visual states" worry raised earlier the same
night. That was true and remains true of the SOLID frames — for a single-hue pool, armour's L1/L2 are both
`$GLED,4,9,9`, L3/L4 both `4,4,9`, L5/L6 both `4,4,4` — but it was an argument FOR keeping the blink, not
against the seven levels: the blink is what makes adjacent levels distinguishable, so it is carrying real
information rather than decorating. Red stays critical-only on its own (L1 and nowhere else), which is what
the original brief actually wanted, and it holds without also reserving the blink. The two `PROPOSED` scenes
built on the stage to compare the alternatives have been deleted now that they are answered.

**Drain direction, fixed and verified the same evening.** Tony tested native Supremacy alongside ours and
found our bar emptying backwards: *"led 3 is the last one to be lost. led 1 turns off first"*. We lit the
FIRST n LEDs. Reversed into a single helper (`poolgauge._lit_leds`) and confirmed on the gun for both pools:
armour `purple purple purple → · purple purple → · · purple`, health `green green green → · green green →
· · yellow → · · RED` — so the colour shift rides the LAST surviving segment, which is also what stock does.
Tony: *"direction matches native"*.

Worth naming the pattern, because it is now three for three tonight. The empty colour token, the burst's
resting brightness, and the drain direction were all **beliefs with passing tests defending them** — the
drain test was literally called `test_segment_frame_lights_only_the_first_n_leds`. A test written from the
same assumption as the code does not check the assumption, it ratifies it. Two of the three were only
findable on hardware, and this one needed the STOCK MODE running beside ours, which no tooling here does.
The standing lesson from `measurement-discipline` holds and gained a corollary: when the gun is the only
authority, a green suite is evidence about our consistency, not about the gun.

## 2026-09-09 (late) · The site, after the rewrite: nine guards that could not see their own fault

The manual site work ran the whole evening past the rebuild entry above. Two review teams, two
polish-loop iterations and a fact-check swept it; what follows is only the part worth carrying.

**The count that matters: NINE guards, written to catch a specific fault, could not catch that
fault.** They are all one shape, and the shape is not "a bad regex". It is *the guard reads an
artefact instead of exercising the behaviour*:

1. The redirect check read `_redirects` as text and never made a request. Seven of nine live pages
   sat in an infinite 301 loop in production while the suite said green.
2. The markdown-twin check asserted "the file exists and has an H1". Rewriting every twin to
   `# Wrong page` passed.
3. The block-marker check was `^`-anchored, so `- [download] ...` mid-line shipped a raw DSL marker.
4. The build wrote every page, THEN exited 1, leaving bad output for the next `git commit -a`.
5. The arsenal check verified the three fields I had just edited and none of the other eight, which
   were Open BRX's rebalanced UI bars being published as Callsign wire values.
6. The manifest asserted `problems: []` on a file that could only exist when problems was empty.
7. The link check excluded `github.com/tony99nyr`, which was exactly the URL that 404s, because the
   repo is private. That link was in the footer of all twelve pages.
8. The LED colour guard asked whether a word appeared ANYWHERE on the page. "dark", "purple" and
   "orange" were all already there, so flipping `GUN_DEFAULT` to any of them stayed green.
9. **The gate had one viewport.** Every step ran at 1280x900, so the assertion checking that an
   anchor clears the sticky header could not fail for its own bug: the desktop header is 64px and
   the margin was 72px. The bug only exists at 390px, where the header is 184px. Half the suite's
   phone claims were unfalsifiable. This one is the worst because the blind spot was the HARNESS,
   not the check, and it hid the quality of every other phone assertion.

Two of these were fixes for earlier items on this same list, which is the real lesson: **fixing an
F40 is where the next F40 comes from.** The rule that actually works is the one the bench uses.
Break the thing, watch the guard fail, then fix it. Every guard in `site/` has now been broken on
purpose and observed failing; the ones that could not be broken locally (Cloudflare's `_redirects`)
got a local mirror so they could be.

**A guard's blind spot belongs in the guard.** `site/lib/led-facts.mjs` reads `SHIELD_COLOUR`,
`ARMOUR_COLOUR`, `GUN_DEFAULT` and the drain direction out of the live Python, and fails the site
build when the page disagrees. It now says in a comment what it does NOT prove: that the page agrees
with the code, never that the code is right. The bar drained backwards for a day and a half with a
passing test defending it; had the page been written then, this guard would have certified the wrong
direction just as happily. Only Tony at the bench settles that.

**Published falsehoods found and fixed**, each one a thing a reader would have acted on: the arsenal
published rebalanced UI bars as captured wire values (Assault Rifle damage 8 where its own frame says
9) under a heading reading "The complete Callsign arsenal"; 1,211 machine transcripts were published
as confirmed meanings, including `V116` which `F43` already recorded as wrong; the quickstart told
people to run a match at `volume=69`, the value measured inaudible across a field; the platform page
denied field games that happened; every page linked a private repository and credits claimed the
notes were public; and the manual promised "official PDFs are linked" while linking none.

**Provenance, and where the line falls.** Tony's instruction was that the manual states facts and does
not narrate its own corrections. The cut that survives contact: *reader-relevant provenance goes on the
page, process provenance goes in the log*. "The teal shield bar has never appeared on a gun" earns its
place because it changes what a reader goes looking for. "Our tests agreed with our code" does not,
because it changes nothing they can see or do.

**Suites:** 62 site steps at both widths, 19 docs and derived-data tests. The site is 12 plain-markdown
pages, a 597-line generator, and a 408-line gate. Nothing under `mcp/brx_mcp/` was touched all night.

### 2026-09-09 (bench, late — Tony + Tactix-E20D) — ⭐ `$LIFE` TAKES NEGATIVES: host-side damage-over-time is real. And a host kill is invisible to the node

Bench A1, the first rung of [`bench-queue-2026-09-09.md`](../bench-queue-2026-09-09.md) and the cheapest in
the queue: ~10 minutes, one gun, no rig. Six findings, three corrections to `protocol/brx-protocol.md`, one
🔴 filed.

**The question.** No `$SIR` function is a damage-over-time (§5, measured), so poison/burn/bleed can only exist
if the NODE can drain a pool over BLE. `$LIFE` and `$BUMP` were documented "additive, clamped at the pool max,
never an absolute set" — and **grep found zero experiment-log citations for either command, in either
direction**, while `mc/types.py` syphon already depended on the claim. So the probe measured the documented
behaviour and the undocumented one in the same sitting.

**Results.**
1. **`$BUMP` is inert.** `-5` on full HP and `+5` on armour at 61: nothing, either direction.
2. **`$LIFE` works and is additive** — armour 61 → 66. First measurement we have ever had. **S14 syphon's
   foundation is sound.**
3. **`$LIFE` self-emits `$HP`.** The doc said writes do not. It does — so a node reads the result instead of polling.
4. ⭐ **`$LIFE` accepts NEGATIVES and drains** — `$LIFE,0,-5,0,*` took armour 66 → 61. **DoT is buildable**
   with no firmware change and no IR per tick (S16).
5. **A negative is per-pool, floors at 0, and does NOT spill.** `$LIFE,0,-100,0,*` on armour 61 left armour 0
   and **HP untouched at 45** — it does not cascade shield → armour → HP the way IR damage does. A host applying
   damage walks the pools itself.
6. 🔴 **A lethal negative really kills, and says so with the wrong frame.** `$LIFE,-45,0,0,*` at HP 45 produced a
   real death — Tony: *"its dead, headset green blinking, trigger does nothing"* — and the wire agreed
   independently: 15 `$BUT,0,1`/`0,0` pairs with **no `$ALCD`**, the documented dead-gun signature. But the gun
   emitted **`$LCD,0,0,0,0,32,192,*` and never `$HP,0,0,0`**. The frame shape SWAPS on the lethal write. Since
   `engine.js _onHp`, `stage.py` and `spec/node.md` all book a death from `$HP,0` — deliberately, as anti-cheat —
   a host-inflicted kill would be invisible to our own stack. Filed **F64** ⚠️ **and that consequence was WRONG,
   corrected 2026-09-10 by a code reviewer:** `engine.js:1264` books a death inside `case 'LCD'`
   (`hp === 0 && this.alive`), and `stage.py:1059` routes `$LCD` through the same `_on_pools`. I had read
   `_onHp`, seen death booked from `$HP`, and never opened the `$LCD` handler. The WIRE fact stands; the
   inference from it did not. What survives is narrower: the `$LCD` path skips `_onHp`, so a host kill is booked
   with no `hit_taken` fact and no attribution, which S16 must resolve.

**The control that settles finding 6 arrived by accident.** A `loopback.py` run fired its six calibration
words with the gun sitting in board B's beam path, and killed it: six `$HIR,4,0,42,2,20,0,0` at 1 s intervals,
70 → 50 → 30 → 10 → 35/0 → 15/0 → 0. That IR death emitted **`$HP,0,0,0,*` and THEN
`$LCD,0,0,0,0,32,384,*`** — both frames, in that order. The `$LIFE` death twenty minutes earlier emitted the
`$LCD` **alone**. Same gun, same session, same arming: the missing `$HP` is specific to the HOST write, not a
property of dying. That is a stronger claim than the one F64 was filed on, and it cost nothing.

**It also explains the loopback's "MARGINAL LINK" verdict, which was not about the link.** 6/6 decoded but
2/6 bit-exact reads as bad aim or distance; in fact the GUN was occluding the receiver and absorbing the shots
meant for it. A rig-trust gate that fails because the subject is standing in the beam is worth knowing about:
check what is between the boards before re-aiming them. And keep the gun off that axis, or calibration kills it.

**The control is the whole reason any of this counts, and the first reading was nearly a false negative.**
`$BUMP,-5` showed no change against a `$QUERY` reading 45/70 — which is also exactly what `$PSET` had just
configured. A null result read through an instrument that might be echoing config is not a result. So: fire one
real IR word from board B (`ir-emit`, damage 9, team 0 against the gun's `$TID,1`), watch `$HIR,4,0,42,0,9,0,0`
and `$HP,45,61,0` land, then re-`$QUERY` — `$LCD,45,61` proved the read tracks LIVE pools, not config, and only
then did the `$BUMP` null mean anything. Same lesson as the A17 shape picks and the F37 retraction: **check the
control before the result.**

**And the sheet's own ordering was wrong, which is worth keeping.** A1 as written did `+5` first as the positive
control. On a full pool an additive-clamped write is indistinguishable from an inert one, so the control could
not have moved. Negative first, then `+5` on the pool the negative just depleted, makes both readings live. The
sheet is corrected.

**Also decoded, unasked:** `$QUERY`'s reply is not "undecoded" as the doc claimed. The array is
`$QUERY,0,1,45,70,70,V3I,1,9,R01,…` = configured HP / armour / max armour / voice pack / weapon damage / fire
sound, and the `$LCD` beside it carries the LIVE pools. That distinction is what made finding 1 readable.

**Housekeeping:** the LED session's `stage --ir COM8` server (started 19:13) was still holding the gun AND COM8
after its session closed, so the gun never appeared in a scan — the "stale server silently owns a gun" gotcha,
second time this week. Killed it; both freed. Two `brx_mcp` processes from 10:19 this morning are also still up.

### 2026-09-09 (bench, night — Tony + Tactix-E20D + the IR rig) — ⭐⭐ SIMULATED RECOIL IS REAL: t21 = ceiling, t22 = floor, `$ALCD` token 2 = live accuracy, and a miss emits magnitude 0

Bench C1 / **F46 CLOSED**. The vendor manual has described a simulated-recoil accuracy model since the beginning and
every capture we or JEDGE ever took showed 100/100, so the archive's verdict was "the model is dormant in app play".
It is not dormant. **Stock 100/100 makes the ceiling equal the floor, which disables it.** Open the gap and it runs.

**The mechanism, all measured on the AR at t14 = 100 ms, holding the trigger through a 32-round magazine:**

| t21 / t22 | `$ALCD` token 2 across the burst |
|---|---|
| 100 / 100 (stock) | 100 for all 32 rounds. No drift at all |
| 100 / **0** | 80 · 60 · 40 · 40 · 40 · 20 · 20 · **0**, then 0 for the rest |
| 100 / **50** | 90 · 90 · 90 · 90 · 90 · 80 · 70 · 70 · 70 · 60 · **50**, then 50 for 22 more rounds |

- **t21 is the ceiling** — t21 = 0 armed token 2 at 0 *before a shot was fired*.
- **t22 is the floor** — 50 stopped dead at 50; 0 went to 0. Never below.
- **`$ALCD` token 2 is the LIVE ACCURACY**, one value per round. `brx-protocol.md` called it "the gun's audio level";
  that was wrong, and it is corrected. It **resets to the ceiling on reload**.
- **The drop is proportional**, about one fifth of the ceiling-to-floor range per shot: both runs took ~5-6 shots to
  reach their floor despite very different gaps. So the gap sets the severity, not a fixed step.
- **A shot below the ceiling emits IR magnitude 0** — the manual's miss. Whole words recovered: at floor 0, 16 × `mag=0`
  vs 12 × `mag=9`; at floor 50, 4 × `mag=0` vs 12 × `mag=9`. **Probabilistic, not a threshold** (misses occur at floor 50).
- **A native time-based recovery races the drop, so `t14` is the throttle.** At t22 = 0, single shots ~2 s apart held a
  flat 80 forever; a held trigger reached 0 in eight rounds. Same floor, opposite outcome, only the spacing differed.
  So weapon feel now has a third lever set: **t21 ceiling · t22 floor · t14 how fast you get there** (`weapon-design.md` §4.4).

**What a miss does, with a same-aim control minutes apart.** Three magnitude-9 words: two `$HIR` (sensors 2 and 4) and
`$HP` 70→61→52. Three magnitude-0 words: **zero events**. But the gun is plainly receiving them — it **vibrates**, plays
its `$PSET` `missShotHit` sound, and **flashes the headset green exactly like a hit**. So a miss is delivered to the
PLAYER natively and well, and to our SOFTWARE not at all. Two consequences worth more than the finding itself:
**(1)** our accuracy stat is accidentally correct, because it counts shots from the shooter's `$ALCD` and hits from the
victim's `$HIR`, so a miss lands right by never arriving; **(2)** nothing can REACT to a miss on either phone — filed
around F68/S17.

**F68 🔴, found by following the flash.** The headset flash on a miss **wipes the painted team colour and it stays dark**
(Tony, watching a blue-painted gun: green flash, then dark, permanently). Our repaint is gated on `dmg > 0`
(`engine.js:1494`, mirrored `stage.py:1101`), and a miss never sets `dmg`, so the node never learns. **The first miss of a
life removes that player's team identity** — and it is invisible today only because misses cannot happen at 100/100. It
would have shipped with S17.

**`H06` answered on the way past:** Tony heard `missShotHit` by ear and it is a bullet near-miss, correctly slotted.
Half of F45 closed. Spaced misses play it every time; three back-to-back played it once (F67c).

**And it cost `$SIR` fn 23 its label.** That row called fn 23 an "audio suppressor" citing token 2 dropping 100 → 0.
Token 2 is accuracy, so **that number never evidenced the audio claim at all**. The silence was *heard* and stands; the
accuracy drop was *measured* and stands; what is gone is one reading doing duty for two claims (F66). Note fn 23 forces
accuracy below any `t22` a weapon ships, which no firing walk can do — a real debuff primitive.

## Method, because this run was nearly a confident wrong answer three times

1. **Tony's correction saved the result.** My first three rungs fired SINGLE SHOTS ~2 s apart — maximum recovery time,
   the one condition where accuracy never leaves the ceiling. They produced a clean A-B-A control and the conclusion
   "t22 is inert". He pushed back twice ("the mechanics of the missed shots was from holding down and using full auto
   right?", "if we are doing spaced shots then we may not be activating a firmware feature") and the held-trigger rung
   found the whole mechanism. **A control proves you measured something repeatably; it cannot tell you the feature was
   switched on.**
2. **Three null readings were rig artefacts, each caught by a control.** A `mag=9` control fired between miss tests
   registered nothing twice, because the gun was out of board B's beam — once because I had asked for exactly that during
   the loopback. Without the control those nulls read as "magnitude 0 does not register" and "the LED does not change".
3. **The loopback's "MARGINAL LINK" verdict was not about the link.** 6/6 decoded, 2/6 bit-exact — because the gun was
   parked in the beam, occluding the receiver, and it took six magnitude-20 words and died (`gotchas.md` now warns).
4. **A stitched IR word can be parity-valid and wrong in the field under test.** Rig qualification threw up `proto=4`,
   `proto=1`, `mag=137`, `mag=41` as second candidates that all passed parity — exactly the fields F46 and F63 measure.
   Only `WORD`/unambiguous-`STITCH` lines were counted here (F12, raised to 🟠).

### 2026-09-10 (bench, early — Tony + Tactix-E20D + grenade + rig) — 🏔️ THE HILL IS A COMPLETE NATIVE KotH PRIMITIVE, and one `$SIR` row makes it ours

Went to the grenade looking for a shield lead. Did not find one, and found something better: **capture-the-point
works natively, end to end, and we can read it with a single table row.**

**The beacon carries the OWNER.** Protocol 15, magnitude = the MODE (hill 8 every ~5 s, respawn 6 every ~2.5 s),
team bits = who holds it. **Neutral is team 2.** Measured cleanly after a power-cycle:

| time | word | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 07:59:41 | `proto=15 team=2 mag=8` | neutral |
| 07:59:58 | `proto=0 player=5 team=0 mag=22` | Tony's RED gun claims it |
| 08:00:01 → 08:00:51 | `proto=15 **team=0** mag=8` ×10 | held by red, every beacon |

⚠️ **This retro-corrects the first capture of the session.** A 07:37 capture labelled `hill-neutral` read
`team=1` and I reasoned from "neutral = team 1". It was not neutral: it was a hill Tony had already claimed as
blue in a native game. **Neutral is team 2.** Nothing else in the session depended on it, but the label was
wrong for twenty minutes and the correction came from Tony saying *"you captured the grenade IR when it was
claimed by me in native mode"* -- not from the wire.

⚠️ **[CORRECTED LATER THE SAME SESSION — see the 2026-09-10 addendum below. An owned hill CAN be retaken; a
shotgun did it. The paragraph as written was wrong and is kept for provenance.]** An owned hill cannot be
re-claimed by shooting it. A red gun firing on the blue-held hill changed nothing
over a 60 s capture; the identical shot took a *neutral* hill on the first round. So hills do not change hands on
a hit and **a contest mechanic is missing** — any KotH design must supply it (neutralise step, dwell, host rule).

**One row makes a hosted game see it (answers `bench-grenade.md` Q3, explains B23).** Adding
`$SIR,15,0,,24,0,0,1,,*` made beacons appear immediately as `$HIR,<sensor>,15,0,<owner>,8,0,0`, no pool change.
Station words "do nothing in a host-driven game" because **our compiled table has no protocol-15 row and the
firmware discards an unmatched cell in silence** — the F40/F60 shape again, third instance this week.

**F69 🔴, the live hazard.** A hill also puts a **standard `proto=0 mag=8` damage word** on the air, and that
one our ordinary `$SIR,0,0,,1` row applies in full. Tony, flipped to the non-owning team, was killed by an
unattended hill in ~106 s (8 a tick, 70 armour + 45 HP to zero) with nothing in the event stream naming the
cause. Today a hosted game takes the punishment while being blind to the objective.

**Tony's design, and it is two rows rather than a build.** *"it should grant shield to those in the hill on the
team which captured it... the hill shouldn't kill you when you are in it. opponents could die while trying to
claim it."* The firmware's polarity gate does the sorting for free: damage functions land only from an enemy,
grants only from your own team, and both grenade words carry the OWNER's team. So `<0,0>` on fn 1 punishes
intruders (already true), and `<15,0>` on a grant function (fn 11/18) shields holders. Not yet proven — the one
attempt was void (no beacons were reaching the gun) and `$SIR` row REPLACEMENT is itself unverified, see below.

**Unresolved, and left honest rather than explained:**
- **The damage word appeared in one window and not another.** 60 s of an owner holding the hill produced beacons
  only, no `proto=0`. We never had board A and the gun watching the SAME window — the missing control. Until we
  do, "an enemy-held hill emits damage, an owned one does not" is a story, not a finding.
- **Can a `$SIR` row be replaced in place?** Re-sending `<15,0>` with fn 11 produced no `$HIR` at all, which is
  equally explained by "the cell still holds the old enemy-only fn 24 and Tony was now the same team". `$CLEAR`
  is the only documented way to wipe the table.
- **A native game DROPS the BLE link and does not answer `$QUERY`.** So native behaviour cannot be instrumented
  from inside without `$PHONE`, which ends the native condition. Board A is the only non-invasive instrument.
  Worth knowing for the node: if a player can reach a native game from the on-gun menu mid-match, the node loses
  its gun.

**Bonus confirmations from native traffic, unasked:** the native gun emitted `mag=22 crit=1` twice — **the crit
bit is really set in play** (F62) — and `proto=0 player=6 team=0 mag=0`, a **magnitude-0 MISS in a native game**,
which independently confirms the simulated-recoil model found hours earlier with no configuration of ours.

**Aim was the single biggest source of bad data tonight: five nulls, all of them board B pointing at the wrong
thing**, each caught only by an embedded control. The emitter's mounting is worth more than any remaining rung.

### 2026-09-10 (addendum) — 🔁 RETRACTION: an owned hill CAN be retaken, and the reason is STILL not settled

Two corrections to the entry above, both mine, both the same mistake made twice.

**1. "An owned hill cannot be re-claimed by shooting it" is WRONG.** A shotgun retook a red hill for blue with a
single word, captured on the rig as `proto=0 team=1 mag=70` — the shotgun's `t12` extraHeadsetDamage, not its
`t5` of 45. Confirmed over the following beacons. The earlier failure used a NATIVE weapon at mag 22 under native
config, so two variables differed and I generalised from the wrong one.

**2. The replacement explanation is ALSO not safe.** Tony's hypothesis was that capture needs the short-range
extra-headset word (`$WEAP` t1=2), and an AR control failed to retake the hill with 4 verified `mag=9` words on
the air. But `docs/reference/grenade.md` §"King of the Hill" already documents a **CHARGE** mechanic from a prior
session — *"each shot adds charge up to a max; to retake, the other team must fire at least as many rounds back
into it"* — under which one shotgun word (70) simply out-charges four AR rounds (36) with no headset word
involved. **Weapon block and magnitude both differed. Again.** F70 now carries both explanations and the
discriminator (empty a 32-round AR magazine into an owned hill).

**The process failure is the finding worth keeping.** `reference/grenade.md` already held the charge mechanic and
was not read before concluding — twice in one session, the same shape: a two-variable comparison read as
single-cause, published, and then caught. The house rule ("read the manual/reference page before digging") exists
for exactly this, and the cost of skipping it here was two retractions in ninety minutes.

### 2026-09-10 (bench, cont.) — ✅ CAPTURE IS CHARGE: any weapon takes a point, and my headset-word hypothesis is dead

Tony, back at the bench, with the observation that settled it before a shot was fired: **"in the native game all
players can capture the hill."** Native players carry ordinary weapons, so a mechanism only three weapons possess
could never be what capture runs on. The test then confirmed it.

**Clean run, our config throughout.** Grenade power-cycled to a verified NEUTRAL hill (`proto=15 team=2 mag=8`).

| step | what | result |
|---|---|---|
| claim | **ONE** AR round, blue (`proto=0 team=1 mag=9`) | hill → `team=1`, holding ~9 of charge |
| contest | re-armed RED, emptied a magazine | first beacon **2.3 s later already `team=0`** (13 AR rounds on the air), red for the remaining 48 s |

**A weapon with NO extra-headset block retook an owned point.** Capture is CHARGE, confirming what
`reference/grenade.md` documented from a prior session.

**Deliberately claimed with one AR round rather than the shotgun**, so the hill held only ~9 charge: a full
magazine failing against 9 would have refuted charge outright. It did not fail.

**The hypothesis was never needed and the evidence never supported it.** One shotgun word is 70 of charge against
four AR rounds at 36, so last night's shotgun-succeeds/AR-fails result is exactly what charge predicts. Weapon
block AND magnitude differed; I picked the wrong one. **Third time in this session**, and this one had already
been sharpened by a review pass into "charge is the incumbent, the headset word carries the burden of proof" —
which turned out to be the right frame, one rung before the bench said so.

**Still unmeasured: the exchange rate.** 13 rounds is an upper bound, not a measurement — beacons arrive only
every ~5 s and he was firing faster. Charge-per-magnitude, whether a full hill costs proportionally more, and the
max charge are all open, and a mode that wants to tune contest difficulty needs them (fire *n*, WAIT for a
beacon, repeat).

**What this unblocks:** K1 objectives no longer need special weapons. Any player can take a point, which is what
makes King of the Hill and Domination playable for a whole team rather than for three loadouts.

### 2026-09-10 (bench, cont.) — 📐 THE HILL'S EXCHANGE RATE IS 1:1 AND LINEAR

Follow-on from the charge finding, run with a one-round-then-wait protocol because the previous "13 rounds" was
an upper bound rather than a measurement (beacons only arrive every ~5 s, and he had been firing faster).

Each run started from a **power-cycled neutral hill** (charge 0, `team=2` verified on the receiver), was seeded
with a counted number of AR rounds from one team, then contested **one round at a time** by the other, with a
beacon read between every shot. Round counts confirmed on the wire by `$ALCD`, not by eye.

| hill seeded with | rounds to retake | `$ALCD` |
|---|---|---|
| 1 AR round (9) | **1** | 32 → 31 |
| 5 AR rounds (45) | **5** | 32 → 27 |

**Charge accumulates and costs the attacker exactly what the defender invested.** A hill holding 9 fell to a
single round; a hill holding 45 survived one, two, three, four and fell on the fifth. So this is a self-tuning
objective mechanic: a lightly-touched point changes hands instantly, a defended one is expensive, and **no host
rules are needed to make holding a point mean something**.

⚠️ **What this does NOT settle: rounds versus magnitude.** Both sides fired `mag=9` throughout, so it measured
rounds AT EQUAL MAGNITUDE. The earlier shotgun result points the other way — one `mag=70` word took a hill that
four `mag=9` rounds had not — which would make magnitude the real currency and heavy weapons better at capturing.
That is a live balance lever and the probe is five minutes: seed 5 AR rounds, contest with one shotgun word.

**Also noted, and it is a design constraint rather than a bug:** with a `$SIR,15,0,,24` row loaded, every beacon
registers as a hit on the gun — green flash, hit sound, vibration, every ~5 s for as long as anyone stands there
— and Tony reports it **queues**: he switched the grenade off and the acknowledgements kept arriving. Any mode
that reads beacons must pick that row's `<soundID>` deliberately and must not drive per-beacon feedback.

### 2026-09-10 (bench, cont.) — ⚖️ THE CURRENCY IS MAGNITUDE: a weapon's capture power equals its damage

The discriminator for the one thing the 1:1 result could not separate. Both earlier runs fired `mag=9` on both
sides, so they measured rounds AT EQUAL MAGNITUDE and could not tell round-count from magnitude apart.

From a power-cycled neutral: seeded **5 AR rounds (45)**, verified blue, then re-armed RED with the **shotgun**
and fired **ONE shell**. `$ALCD` 6→5, one shell — and the next five beacons all read `team=0`.

| seeded | contested with | totals | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 AR (9) | 1 AR | 9 v 9 | flipped |
| 5 AR (45) | 5 AR | 45 v 45 | flipped on the 5th |
| 5 AR (45) | **1 shotgun (70)** | 45 v **70** | **flipped on one shell** |

**Charge accumulates in MAGNITUDE and the higher total owns the point.** One rule now covers every hill reading
taken across both days, including 2026-09-09's shotgun-succeeds/AR-fails result: the shotgun's `mag=70` word
out-charged the hill while four AR rounds at 36 did not. That was always charge. Tony called it from the chair
— *"took control on 1 hit. its magnitude"* — before I had finished reading the capture.

**Why this is the good answer for mode design.** A weapon's capture power equals its damage with no extra stat:
a shotgun or rocket seizes a point in one hit, a rifleman needs a burst, and retaking a defended point costs
exactly what the defender invested. The objective economy falls straight out of the weapon balance we already
tune, and King of the Hill needs no host rules of its own to be fair.

**Still unmeasured:** the max charge — whether a hill caps, and how long a full one takes to build.

### 2026-09-10 (bench, cont.) — 📣 THE GRENADE ANNOUNCES A CAPTURE: a transition PAIR, and it is why guns say "hill captured"

Tony asked the question that found it: *"does the grenade emit an IR when its hit that the guns receive and
understand? the guns seem to know when a control point is captured in native mode."* They do, and the answer was
already in tonight's logs — **I had dismissed it as stitcher noise.**

At the instant a grenade changes hands it emits, in the same burst as the capturing shot and before the periodic
beacon resumes:

| word | bits | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `proto=15 team=2 mag=53` | `1111000000100011010100001` | the state being LEFT (neutral, in these runs) |
| `proto=15 team=<new> mag=50` | `1111000000010011001000010` (team 1) | the owner being ENTERED |

**Byte-identical across three separate captures**, two of the three decoding unambiguously, always in that order.
Stitcher noise does not reproduce the same two bit patterns three times with coherent team fields — which is what
I should have checked before writing them off. That is now twice in this session that a real signal was filed as
an artefact and recovered only because Tony asked a question that made me look again.

**Consequences.** Guns are TOLD about a capture rather than inferring it from a beacon whose team changed — and a
hosted game with the protocol-15 row hears the same announcement, so a node can fire a capture callout at the
moment it happens instead of up to 5 s later.

**And a real design limit, stated because someone will otherwise spec it:** capture PROGRESS is not on the wire.
No charge value has ever been observed; the beacon carries owner and mode only. Progress lives inside the
grenade, so a hosted mode learns the discrete transition and **cannot draw a capture bar**.

**Scope, honestly.** `mag=50` is confirmed for a team-to-team takeover as well (a blue hill retaken by red emitted
`team=0 mag=50` with no `53`). `mag=53` appeared in all three neutral→team captures and not in the team→team one,
but those windows were thin, so its absence there is weak evidence rather than a finding. Whether `53` means "was
neutral" or "the outgoing state" generally is open.

⚠️ **CORRECTED LATER THE SAME DAY (2026-09-10 evening) — see the "rung S / capture proven" entry below.**
"In the same burst as the capturing shot" was wrong. A clean BLE capture of a live shot-to-capture sequence
shows `mag=50` arriving ~50 ms after the shot and `mag=53` arriving a full beacon cycle (~5 s) later — two
separate events, not one pair. The "same burst" reading was an artefact of the ESP32 IR rig stitching
overlapping transmissions into one capture window; that explanation of why the rig could never see them apart
is still correct, but the timing claim built on top of it was not. Corrected at source in
`protocol/brx-ir-protocol.md` and `docs/bench-grenade.md` as well.

✅ **ALSO SETTLED THE SAME EVENING:** whether `53` means "was neutral" specifically was left open here on the
grounds that the team-to-team window above was thin. A later run on a continuous BLE stream (gun armed and
listening throughout, not a thin IR window) repeated the blue→red result cleanly — `mag=50` with no `mag=53`
anywhere — while a neutral→blue capture on the SAME connection carried both. **`mag=53` means the point WAS
NEUTRAL; `mag=50` announces the incoming owner on every capture.** See the entry below for the frames and the
design consequence.

### 2026-09-10 (bench, cont.) — 👻 THE PHANTOM-HIT LOOP: a gun replaying an IR event forever, with nothing in the air

The U11′ sweep (F73) went strange — inconsistent hit counts, several sounds per shot, and words decoding on the
wrong protocol. I diagnosed rig geometry twice (reflections, then a stuck emitter) and was wrong twice. **Tony had
the answer early:** *"i think it also queues the events on the tagger"*, and later *"i literally havent moved. im
just sitting here"* when I was still blaming aim.

**What it actually is.** The gun emitted `$HIR,0,0,42,0,20,0,0` + `$HP` **every 5.07 s, indefinitely**, while
**board A recorded ZERO bursts across an 18 s capture**. Nothing was transmitting: the grenade was off, the
emitter idle, the operator stationary. The gun had **latched an IR event and was replaying it internally**.

| | |
|---|---|
| survives | grenade off, IR-silent room, **three `$PLAYX,0,*`** (audio quiets, the event continues) |
| cleared by | **`$SPAWN,,*`** — the same command that clears the `$SIR` fn-23 state |
| operator sees | one hit, one hissing sound, on a loop, indistinguishable from being shot |

**Filed F74 🔴, and it is a match-day problem, not a bench curiosity:** a latched gun manufactures hits that MC
will score. Accuracy, kill attribution and hit feedback all corrupt, and nothing in the node or MC can separate a
replayed `$HIR` from a real one. A node-side guard is plausible — an identical `$HIR` repeating at a fixed period
with no `$ALCD` from any shooter is not a real hit — but nothing implements it.

**Method lesson, which is the reusable part.** Three separate explanations were proposed and rejected before the
right one, and the thing that settled it was **the receiver, not more reasoning**: pointing board A at an
apparently-busy room and getting zero bursts proved instantly that the gun was inventing the traffic. *Check
whether the signal exists before explaining the signal.* The repo's receiver-first convention exists for
"what does X emit"; this is the same tool answering "is anything emitting at all", and it should have been the
first move rather than the fourth.

⚠️ **The F73 sweep is void** and must be re-run: fn 8's silence is the only reading worth keeping, and even that
needs confirming with `$SPAWN` first, a protocol check per trial, and the room IR-quiet.

### 2026-09-10 (bench, cont.) — 🔇 `$SIR` fn 28 REGISTERS WITH ZERO PLAYER FEEDBACK: a control point can be read silently

**F73 closed.** The question, raised by standing in a hill and being flashed and buzzed every 5 s: can a node read
an IR event the player does not experience? **Yes — fn 28.**

**Method, rebuilt after the phantom-hit loop (F74) voided the first attempt.** `$SPAWN` in every arm to clear
latched state; the room IR-quiet (grenade off); and — the fix that mattered — **the cell under test as the ONLY
`$SIR` row**, so a word that mis-decodes to another protocol has no matching row and is discarded silently
instead of producing phantom feedback. Every trial's `$HIR` protocol field checked before its sensory reading
counted.

| fn | registers | sound | headset flash | vibrate |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 8 | ✓ ×3 | none | **yes** | **yes** |
| 24 / 25 / 26 / 27 | ✓ ×3 each | a long grenade-ish clip | ? | ? |
| **28** | **✓ ×3** | **none** | **none** | **none** |

⭐ **fn 28 is the row to ship on protocol 15.** A hosted game reads the hill beacon every ~5 s and the player
feels, hears and sees nothing — which removes the "a control point buzzes at you" objection that opened F73.

**Polarity, measured in both directions.** fn 28 is **enemy-only** under `$GSET` t1=0: three same-team words gave
**zero** registrations, silently rejected. With **t1=1** the gate lifts and the identical words registered
`$HIR,0,5,42,1,20` ×3 — **owner in the team field**. So one `$GSET` bit decides how much a node can see:
FF off = only hills you do NOT own (and you miss your own captures, since the `mag=50` capture word carries the
new owner's team); FF on = every beacon and capture with ownership readable, at the cost of same-team IR
registering elsewhere. **KotH wants FF on**, but that is a mode-level call with consequences beyond the objective.

**The "varied sounds" were one clip all along.** Spacing shots 6 s apart let it play through: a hiss that builds
to a timer and ends in an explosion. Every earlier "different sound each time" was that clip truncated by the
next event. ⚠ Which clip it is remains unidentified — not `A10` (`energyShieldLoop` shipped empty) and not any of
the five `$PSET` hit slots, all of which are 0.36-0.79 s. ⚠ And on fn 27 Tony was certain the first of three was
genuinely a different sound, which a truncated-tail explanation does not cover. Left unexplained rather than
tidied away.

**Tony ran the instrument that mattered.** Every reading here is a human report of sound, light and touch that no
tool in this project can take, and the two that changed the outcome were his: noticing the headset flashed
(killing the audio-queue theory) and reporting "nothing received" on fn 28 when the wire showed three clean
registrations — which is the finding.

---

## 2026-09-10 — F69 was not only a blind spot: the hill was SCORING

Code review of the night's findings (no hardware). The bench had established that an enemy-held grenade hill
drains an intruder with an ordinary `proto=0 mag=8` damage word. The write-up said MC "cannot see why" a player
died. That understated it. Tracing the word through the host engines:

`modes/base.py:shooter_team()` skipped exactly one thing — `token2 == 15`, the beacon. The hill's DAMAGE word is
protocol 0, so it sailed through, was stored by `deathmatch.py:47` as `_last_shot`, and survived to the death
because **`ATTRIB_FUSE_S` (6 s) is wider than the hill's ~5 s period**: the attribution could never go stale, so
every hill tick refreshed it. `_handle_death` then ran `team_score[killer_team] += 1` — and that line fires
whether or not a specific killer gun resolves. **A hill credited its owning team with a kill it did not make.**
Reproduced in `test_a_hill_that_kills_you_scores_for_nobody`: with the guard removed, `team_score == {1: 1, 2: 0}`.

**The fix was already written down, three years of spec ahead of the bug.** A5.1 reserves wire 0 for
"no identity (tutorial arms, **unknown/environmental shooter**) and is never a player"; `compile.py:899` arms
try-outs at `$PSET,0` precisely so a stray hit is "never credited"; MC assigns real players 1–63. Nothing
enforced it in the attribution helpers. Both `shooter_team()` and `shooter_player_id()` now return `None` on
wire 0. A hill is an environmental shooter, so this is the rule applying, not a special case for grenades.

**Why 1024 passing tests could not see it, and this is the real lesson.** Adding the guard broke **30 tests**.
Not because the guard was wrong — because the fixtures were. `sim.py:kill()`, `fake.receive_ir()`, the `hir()`
helpers in four test files and `__main__._hir()` all defaulted the shooter's wire id to **0**.

⚠️ **CORRECTION, same day, caught in review — I wrote that 0 is "a value the platform never puts on the wire",
and that is FALSE.** MC never *assigns* 0; a GUN emits it whenever `$PSET` never landed, which is exactly what
`manual/dev.md` records ("every gun on that capture sat on the default id") and what `compile.py:899` relies on
for try-outs. The distinction matters because it has a cost: **a gun whose `$PSET` failed now has its kills
silently dropped**, where before the guard they were credited to its team via `sole_member_of_team`. I am
keeping the guard anyway — a hill inventing kills corrupts the scoreboard for everyone, while a mis-armed gun
is one already-broken gun — but that is a TRADE, not a free win, and it was being sold as a free win. **The
real fix is upstream and is now F80:** MC should verify `$PSET` landed at arm time rather than letting a gun
into a match with no identity. A single `$HIR` cannot tell a hill from a mis-armed gun, and I am not going to
invent a heuristic that pretends it can. The suite had been proving that the engines correctly credit kills to a shooter
that cannot exist. Fixing the defaults to 1 dropped it to 9, then 0. This is `stage-must-mirror-the-phone`
again: **a fixture that models an impossible frame does not merely miss a bug, it actively certifies the bug as
correct behaviour.** The 30 failures were the suite finally telling the truth.

Also checked and clean: `mc/scoring.py:220` already resolves `shooter_num` through `num_to_pid`, and no player
holds wire 0, so the MC recap path never credited the hill. The gap was the live mode engines only.

Controls kept: `test_a_real_shot_still_scores_when_a_hill_is_also_ticking` interleaves a real wire-1 shooter
with hill words and still books the kill — without it, "the hill scores nothing" would be satisfied just as well
by an engine that had stopped scoring anything.

Filed rather than fixed: **F77** (a replayed hit is indistinguishable from a real one — deliberately no
heuristic, a period-based suppressor would eat real bursts), **F78** (`fake.py` cannot model any of F69–F75),
**F79** (`assert_sir_covers_weapons` is blind to a missing protocol-15 cell).

### Review pass, same day: two badges came down and a decode count was wrong

Three reviewers over the night's commits. What they caught, and what I did:

**The beacon period was written two ways in one file.** `reference/grenade.md` gave `~5 s` in its beacon table
(line 100, matching every other doc) and `~3-4 s` in the King-of-the-Hill prose (line 174) — inside the very
paragraph edited hours earlier for the F76 capture-currency fix. The `~3-4 s` was never measured. Corrected to
the bench figure, with the correction stated in place rather than silently swapped.

**HANDOFF contradicted itself inside one bullet**, saying "WHAT captures a point is unsettled" in the same
paragraph that cites F70, which settled exactly that. A fossil left standing when the text around it was
rewritten — and HANDOFF is the file the next session reads first.

**The protocol doc was still telling implementers to ship fn 24.** F73 settled that fn 28 registers with no
sound, no flash and no vibration, while fn 24 fires a long clip every ~5 s for as long as anyone stands in the
hill. `bench-grenade.md` marked its early fn-24 guidance as superseded; `brx-ir-protocol.md`, the ground-truth
doc, did not, and presented fn 24 flat as current guidance. Anyone reading the protocol spec alone would have
shipped the objectionable row.

**Two ✅ badges came down to 🟡.** "THE EXCHANGE RATE IS 1:1 AND LINEAR" rests on **two points** (seeded 1 →
retaken with 1; seeded 5 → retaken with 5). Two points define a line by construction, not by measurement —
nothing measured separates 1:1-linear from any curve through (1,1) and (5,5). "THE CURRENCY IS MAGNITUDE"
rests on **one** discriminating trial, and F76 stands against it. Both keep a 🟡 rather than dropping further
because the reasoning is genuinely discriminating (a rounds model predicts the shell should have lost, and it
won) — but a session that has already retracted four single-trial conclusions should not be stacking
checkmarks on n=1. **"CAPTURE IS CHARGE, any weapon takes a point" keeps its ✅**: that one is multiply sourced
— the bench run, `reference/grenade.md`'s prior hardware-confirmed mechanic, and Tony's own native play.

**The decode count was wrong, and recounting it changed the claim.** `bench-grenade.md` said "84 clean decodes,
three values only". A reviewer noticed the components did not add up, so I recounted from the capture logs
rather than reconciling the arithmetic. Unambiguous proto-15 decodes across every session:

| mag | count | what |
|---|---|---|
| 8 | 96 | hill |
| 6 | 63 | respawn |
| 53 / 50 | 4 / 3 | capture announcement |
| 56 | 1 | boot — a WHOLE word, 2026-09-04 |
| 55 | 1 | stitched only, at a power-cycle |
| 2 | 1 | stitched only, and it carries `player=42`, which no other beacon does |

So the total was wrong AND "three values only" was wrong — there are seven. The claim it was supporting
survives, because it never depended on the count: 8 and 6 have **96 and 63 repeats** behind them, and the
one-off values are single stitched decodes with a visible artefact signature. `brx-ir-protocol.md`'s separate
"decodes whole 57 times" checked out **exactly** (57 whole `mag=8` words on 09-10).

The lesson is the cheap one: **a number nobody can re-derive from the repo is a number nobody can check.** The
count is now written with its breakdown so the next reader can audit it in one grep.

### Second review pass: the discriminating trial was confounded, and my own fix was sold wrong

Three more reviewers. Four real findings, one of them against the fix I had just committed.

**1. The trial that "proved" magnitude is the currency changed TWO variables.** The shotgun's `mag=70` is its
`t12` **extraHeadsetDamage** — this session's own log says so, in those words — so it comes from a `t1=2`
weapon. "5 AR rounds at 45 lost to one shotgun shell at 70" therefore varied magnitude AND weapon-block
together, and cannot separate *"magnitude is the currency"* from *"an extra-headset word captures out of
proportion"*. That second reading is the hypothesis F70 declares DEAD. **This is the fourth two-variable
comparison in one session**, and the first three all became retractions. Downgraded 🟡 → 🟠 everywhere, with
the clean experiment named: a high-magnitude word from a NON-`t1=2` weapon (a `$WEAP` t5-boosted AR). The AR
magdump still kills *"capture REQUIRES an extra-headset word"* — that part stands. What it never established
is what the exchange is priced in.

It also hands F76 a resolution that fits every reading and that F76 had not listed: the per-weapon round counts
could be right for ordinary weapons while the three extra-headset weapons capture disproportionately. The
reference page's own *"a thrown grenade blast instantly captures 100%"* is that exact shape.

**2. "The higher total owns the point" is falsified by its own table.** Both AR flips landed at **exact
equality** — 9 v 9, and 45 v 45 "flipped on the 5th". A strict *higher* rule predicts neither. The only
strictly-greater run is the confounded shotgun one. What is measured is **the attacker wins ties**.

**3. fn 28 was swept on protocol 5, and I wrote it up as protocol 15.** The whole sweep ran in cell `<5,0>`
with the ESP32 rig; the FF-on registration is `$HIR,0,5,42,1,20` — protocol 5, and that `team=1` is board B's
transmission, not a hill owner. Nobody has loaded `$SIR,15,0,,28` or watched a real ally beacon register. The
function id is very likely the effect and the cell only the key, but that is the assumption. I had promoted it
to ⭐ guidance in the ground-truth protocol doc an hour earlier; now caveated in both places.

**4. "A non-capturing hit emits NOTHING" claims a null the method could not see.** The same session established
that the grenade replies inside the shooter's burst, and that *"any word that only occurs inside a shot's burst
has been invisible to every capture ever taken"* — which is exactly where a hit acknowledgement would live.
Four captures with that blind spot support "no DECODABLE word", not "nothing". Gated on rung B0.

**5. And the one against my own commit: "a value the platform never puts on the wire" is FALSE.** MC never
*assigns* wire 0; a GUN emits it whenever `$PSET` never landed. So the F69 guard has a cost I did not state —
**a mis-armed gun's kills are now silently dropped**, where they used to reach its team by fallback. I am
keeping the guard (a hill inventing kills corrupts the scoreboard for everyone; a mis-armed gun is one already
broken gun) but it is a TRADE and I had written it up as a free win. Opened **F80**: the fix belongs at arm
time, where MC can verify `$PSET` landed, not in attribution where no heuristic can tell a hill from a
mis-armed gun.

**6. F69's "nothing names the cause" was too kind.** `engine.js`'s `$HIR` case excludes only the beacon and
never checks wire 0, so a hill kill latches the owner's team and the DOWN screen renders **"KILLED BY <the
hill's owning team>"**. The scoreboard is fixed and the player is told a specific lie. Opened **F81** — and
deliberately NOT patched here, because a mis-armed gun sends wire 0 too and for that case the team shown is
right. The answer is "killer unknown", which is DOWN-screen copy and belongs to the brx-hud session.

**The pattern worth keeping.** Every one of findings 1-4 is the same error: a conclusion that outran the
method that produced it. Three of them I wrote tonight, one I promoted to a ground-truth doc an hour before it
was caught. The reviewers that found them had no access to my reasoning — only to the files and the logs.
That is the argument for writing the method next to the number, every time.

**Findings 7-12 of the same pass, and one of them is a hole I dug.** My rewrite of the grenade programme
(`5a91b8d`) deleted **rung C** — board A beside the headset AND the gun on BLE, one window, a non-owner in the
hill. That is the *named missing control* for F69, which is still 🔴 and still kills players; this log records
its absence three screens earlier and I then removed the rung that would fix it. It also deleted **rung D**,
which `HANDOFF.md` and `utility-roadmap.md` both still point at. Both restored, labelled as restored.

Added **rung X**, the trial nobody has run and the only one that settles F70 *and* F76 together: fire a
high-magnitude word from a **non-`t1=2`** weapon (boost an AR's `$WEAP` t5 to ~70) into a hill holding 45. If it
takes the point, the currency is magnitude and the per-weapon counts are wrong; if it does not, the
extra-headset block is doing the work and those counts may be right for ordinary weapons. Every capture trial
so far confounds the two because the only high-magnitude word ever fired was a shotgun's `t12`.

🔴 **And a design trap that falls straight out of two measured facts and that nobody had written down: never
put a player on team 2 in a hill mode (F82).** A neutral hill broadcasts team 2, and the polarity gate compares
that against the gun's own `$TID` — so a roster containing team 2 reads every NEUTRAL point as its own. Those
players go deaf to neutral hills under an enemy-only row, and the `proto=0` damage word cannot land on them:
team 2 walks onto any uncaptured point untouched while everyone else is contested. Both inputs are measured;
the consequence is predicted, and rung D would show it directly.

Smaller: "fn 24-27 **additionally** fire a clip" smuggled in flash+vibrate, which both source tables mark `?`
(sound is measured, the rest was never observed). The native callout ids were "confirmed present by catalog
search" — that proves the FILES are on the gun, not that native plays them, in a rung whose own warning calls
the catalog transcripts untrusted; downgraded to candidates. D6 in the bench queue got back the three traps
that voided it the first time ($SPAWN per arm, one row at a time, 3 ft).

---

## 2026-09-10 (evening) — rung S, and the objective's own heartbeat was blocking regen

Tony back at the desk, one gun (`Tactix-E20D`) on BLE, the grenade power-cycled to neutral and facing it.

**Rung S: every hosted callout confirmed BY EAR, and one catalogue entry was wrong.** Driving `$PLAY` over BLE
at `$VOL,80`:

| id | catalogued | actually heard | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| `VA23` | "Control Point Captured." | as catalogued | ✅ |
| `VA22` | "Control Point Lost." | as catalogued | ✅ |
| `VA21` | "Control Point Contested." | as catalogued | ✅ |
| `VA93` | "King of the hill!" | as catalogued | ✅ |
| **`V8Q`** | **"Hill Confirmed"** | **"KILL Confirmed"** | 🔴 **wrong** |
| `VB0N/O/P/Q` | Hill Captured / Contested / Lost! / Moved | as catalogued | ✅ **preferred** |
| `U100`, `U104` | ui ticks | both tick; U100 more clock-like | ✅ `U100` chosen |

**`V8Q` is the catch, and it was worse than a typo.** One letter in a Whisper transcript, and the classifier
filed a KILL-feed line under `voice:objective_hill` — so a hill mode picking callouts BY CATEGORY would have
announced "Kill Confirmed" when someone took a point. It was never wired into an engine, so it was a trap
rather than a live bug. **Fixed at source**, in a new `BY_EAR_CORRECTIONS` table in
`tools/soundbank_classify.py` rather than by editing the generated JSON — a hand-edit there is reverted by the
next regeneration, which is how a fixed bug comes back. This also retires the 2026-09-03 claim that "nothing is
left to confirm by ear": **Whisper transcripts are not sufficient**, and one of them was wrong in a way that
propagated into a category.

Tony's preference, unprompted: the **`VB0*` set** ("like the Halo announcer, and they have dramatic music"),
one female objectives announcer covering all four states, over three male "Control Point" lines. `VB0Q` "Hill
Moved" is real but only meaningful in a ROTATING hill mode — several grenades, the node choosing which is live.
That is now a cheap mode to build and was not on the list.

**Then the real prize: the whole chain, on real hardware.** Armed the gun by hand over BLE with
`$SIR,15,0,,28,0,0,1,,*` (fn 28), `$GSET` t1=1 (see every owner, not only enemies) and **`$TID,1` — NOT 2**,
because a neutral hill broadcasts team 2 and F82 (filed hours earlier, now load-bearing) says a team-2 roster
reads neutral points as its own.

The beacon arrived immediately: `$HIR,4,15,0,2,8,0,0` — protocol 15, player 0, **team 2 = neutral**, mag 8 =
hill, on **sensor 4** (gun body) at desk range. **This settles the caveat added the same afternoon**: fn 28 had
only ever been swept in cell `<5,0>` with the ESP32 rig, so "ship it on protocol 15" was an assumption. It
holds — the cell is the key, the function is the effect. **20+ consecutive beacons, period 5.0 s with no
drift and zero misses** (534.5, 539.4, 544.5, 549.5, 554.5, 559.5 …). Ticking `U100` on each arriving beacon
ran the mode live: grenade transmits → gun registers silently → host reads it over BLE → host plays the cue.

🔴 **AND THE STREAM SHOWED A BUG NOBODY WAS LOOKING FOR.** Every beacon is followed by **`$HP,45,70,0` — an
echo with the pools completely unchanged.** A registration emits `$HP` whether or not anything moved.
`deathmatch.py` restarted the regen idle timer on any non-fatal `$HP`, on the stated premise that *"a non-fatal
$HP = took damage"*. That premise is false, and `regen_delay_s` is **6.0** against a hill period of **5.0**:
`now - last_damage` reset every 5 s and never reached 6. **Anyone standing in a hill never regenerated, for the
whole match, in any regen mode.** Nothing damaged them — the objective's own heartbeat did it. Reproduced:
60 s in a hill = **0 heals**; the same player, same 60 s, no hill = **1 heal**.

**This is the third instance tonight of one shape: a host constant wider than the hill's 5 s period.**
`ATTRIB_FUSE_S` 6.0 made hill attribution permanently fresh (F69). `regen_delay_s` 6.0 makes the idle window
permanently un-reached. **Any timer shorter than an ambient emitter's period never expires.** That belongs on
the checklist for every future objective.

Fixed by measuring what the frame MEANS instead of that it arrived: damage is a **drop in the pools**. Covered
by `test_standing_in_a_hill_does_not_block_health_regen` plus a control (`test_a_real_hit_still_suppresses_regen`)
so "regen works" cannot be satisfied by an engine that ignores damage entirely; verified to fail when reverted.
`hp_values()`'s docstring, which asserted the false premise, is corrected at source.

⭐ **Worth noting which side was right.** `app/src/engine.js:1481` already computed damage from pool deltas and
gated on `dmg > 0`, with a comment about exactly this ("a ZERO-damage `$HP` frame — a heal or regen tick, or a
plain resend"). **The phone had the correct model and the Python mode engine did not** — `stage-must-mirror-the-phone`
running in the opposite direction to usual, and a reminder that the divergence is what matters, not which side
is nominally authoritative.

**Not done:** Tony could not fire, so a live CAPTURE (beacon team flipping under a real shot, with the callout
driven by the transition) is still unproven end to end. The gun was armed to RECEIVE but carried no `$WEAP`, so
its magazine was 0 — my error, and worth remembering for the next run: **a receive-only arm cannot shoot.**

**Rung R (beacon range) is answered, by estimate rather than tape measure.** Close in (desk range) the beacon
is solid, zero misses across 20+ consecutive reads at a clean 5.0 s. At the operator's estimated ~30 ft it
turned intermittent: long dropouts (85 s and 145 s of silence) interleaved with brief runs of clean 5 s
beacons. So the reliable range is well under 30 ft and the useful outer edge is around there. **The hill
beacon reaches further than the respawn station** (documented ~18-20 ft) — worth saying explicitly, a reader
would otherwise assume the two match. ⚠ Not a hard number: one operator estimate, no tape measure, and
`reference/grenade.md` already records the beacon as AIM-SENSITIVE, so orientation was an uncontrolled
variable in this reading. **Design consequence:** presence is not a clean in/out at the range boundary — a
node must not call a player "left the hill" on a single missed beacon; with a 5 s period, a grace of at
least two missed beacons (~12 s) is the floor.

🔴 **AND A SECOND BUG, FOUND THE SAME WAY AS THE FIRST — READING THE STREAM RATHER THAN TRUSTING THE FRAME
COUNT.** One beacon transmission arrived as TWO `$HIR` frames, 14 ms apart, at t=684.5 s:

```
$HIR,4,15,0,2,8,0,0    <- sensor 4, gun body
$HIR,0,15,0,2,8,0,0    <- sensor 0, headset front — SAME transmission
```

Same protocol, same owner, same magnitude, on two different sensors, 14 ms apart — one physical beacon lighting
two receivers on the same gun, and the gun reports both. **Any node that ticks, scores, or counts presence per
`$HIR` will double-count**, and a capture/possession timer driven per-frame would run at roughly double rate.
Needs a dedupe window: same protocol + magnitude + owner within ~100 ms collapses to one beacon. This bites
both sides — the node reading the beacon over BLE, and (once F72 is fixed) the phone's own `$HIR` handling,
since the same duplication would reach `engine.js` unchanged.

## 2026-09-10 (evening, cont.) — CAPTURE PROVEN END TO END, and the capture pair is NOT a same-burst transition

With the gun re-armed and carrying ammo this time (the earlier failure: armed to receive, no `$WEAP`, magazine
0), a full hill capture was read live over BLE, gun on team 1 (blue), a neutral grenade, one AR round:

```
t=41769  $BUT,0,1  + $ALCD,31->30   <- operator's shot
t=41770  $HIR,4,15,0,2,8,0,0        <- last NEUTRAL beacon (team 2)
t=41820  $HIR,4,15,0,1,50,0,0       <- mag 50: NEW OWNER = team 1
t=46780  $HIR,0,15,0,2,53,0,0       <- mag 53: state LEFT = team 2 (neutral)
t=46780  $HIR,4,15,0,1,8,0,0        <- first HILL beacon owned by team 1
t=51720 / 56770 / 61780 / 66790     <- team 1, mag 8, clean 5.0 s cadence, holding
```

Operator confirmed by eye: **the grenade turned BLUE and beeped.** One AR round (magnitude 24) took a neutral
hill. Three things this settles:

1. **The capture-announcement pair is now confirmed CLEANLY OVER BLE**, not only as stitched IR fragments on
   the ESP32 rig. `protocol/brx-ir-protocol.md` downgraded the pair to weak evidence for exactly that reason
   ("why the capture words only ever decode as STITCHED frames") — that hedge is upgraded here: the words
   decode whole and unambiguous on the BLE stream, no stitching involved. The stitched-burst EXPLANATION for
   why the IR rig alone could never see `mag=50` cleanly is still correct; only the evidence tier changes.
2. 🔴 **CORRECTION: the pair is NOT "a transition pair in the same burst as the shot."** That phrasing, written
   earlier the same day (see the 📣 entry above) and copied into `docs/bench-grenade.md` and
   `protocol/brx-ir-protocol.md`, is wrong. Measured here: `mag=50` arrived **50 ms** after the shot; `mag=53`
   arrived **5 s later, on the next beacon cycle** — roughly 5 s apart, not one burst. The "same burst" reading
   was an artefact of the IR rig seeing overlapping transmissions in one capture window. Corrected at source in
   both files. **Design consequence:** a node must not wait for both words before announcing a capture —
   `mag=50` alone is the capture signal and arrives immediately; `mag=53` is a late confirmation of what was
   left behind.
3. **`mag=53` arrived on sensor 0 (headset front)** while the ordinary hill beacons ride sensor 4 (gun body) —
   consistent with the double-`$HIR` finding just above: one grenade transmission, more than one sensor hearing
   it.

The interpretation is now CONFIRMED rather than inferred: **`50` = the incoming owner's team, `53` = the state
being left** (here neutral/team 2).

## 2026-09-10 (evening, cont.) — `mag=53` MEANS THE POINT WAS NEUTRAL, settled by an enemy-to-enemy capture

One question the earlier "GRENADE ANNOUNCES A CAPTURE" entry left open on weak evidence (a thin IR capture
window): does `mag=53` mean "was neutral" specifically, or "the outgoing state" generally? Settled this evening
on a continuous BLE stream — gun armed and listening before, during and after the transition, so an absent
word cannot be blamed on a missed window.

With the hill held by team 1 (blue), `$TID` was live-written to 0 (red) mid-session and the gun shot it on the
very next opportunity:

```
t=291755  $HIR,4,15,0,1,8,0,0     <- blue (team 1) still holds it
t=292145  $BUT,0,1 + $ALCD,28->27 <- the shot
t=292265  $HIR,4,15,0,0,50,0,0    <- mag 50: NEW OWNER = team 0 (red)
t=296835  $HIR,4,15,0,0,8,0,0     <- first hill beacon owned by RED
t=301845  $HIR,4,15,0,0,8,0,0     <- holding, clean 5.0 s
```

**No `mag=53` anywhere in the stream.** The earlier neutral→blue capture, on the same continuous BLE
connection, DID carry `mag=53` right alongside `mag=50`. The two runs differ in exactly one variable — whether
the outgoing state was neutral — which is what settles it: **`mag=53` means the point WAS NEUTRAL; `mag=50`
announces the incoming owner on every capture, neutral or enemy-to-enemy.**

**Design consequence:** a node can tell "captured from neutral" apart from "stolen from an enemy" purely from
whether `mag=53` shows up — exactly the distinction native needs to choose between callouts. And it reconfirms
the earlier timing rule: a node must fire on `mag=50` alone and never wait for `mag=53`, because on an
enemy-to-enemy capture it never arrives at all.

Also worth one line: the live `$TID` write took effect immediately — hit resolution changed on the very next
shot, consistent with the `$TID` row in `protocol/brx-protocol.md`.

## 2026-09-10 (evening, cont.) — a live team switch moves hit resolution but NOT the LEDs, and no mode repaints for it

The same `$TID,1` -> `$TID,0` switch that produced the enemy-to-enemy capture above surfaced a second, more
general hazard while it was happening: **the gun LEDs kept showing the OLD team's colour** even though the
firmware had genuinely moved the player — he captured an enemy-held hill for the new team on the very next
shot. Operator's words, verbatim: *"the gun leds dont show me as red, but i shot the grenade and it switched
to red."*

This is not a protocol bug — `protocol/brx-protocol.md` already documents that a live `$TID` write changes hit
resolution immediately but does not repaint the LEDs, and that held exactly as written. **What was missing is
the consequence for our modes**, and it is a real gameplay hazard, not cosmetic: a player can be on one team
for damage/objective purposes and display as ANOTHER team to themselves and to everyone looking at them —
teammates read each other by LED colour in the field. Checked `docs/led-language.md`: it does not specify a
repaint on team change anywhere today, so this is a genuine gap rather than an unread spec.

**Fix, not yet built:** any mode that changes a player's team mid-match must repaint explicitly right after the
