`$TID` write — blank (`$GLED,,,,5,,,*`) then paint (`$GLED,<colour>,<colour>,<colour>,0,10,,*`, plus `$HLED`),
since a painted colour does not hold on a spawned gun without the blank first. `$SPAWN` also repaints from
`$TID` but is not a free substitute for this — it restores ammo and re-enables the firmware's native breathing
animation as a side effect. **Modes affected: infection** (the single most likely place for this to bite,
since players change team ON infection), any host-driven team swap, and team-based objective modes generally.
Filed as FOLLOWUPS **F86**; a line added to `docs/gotchas.md` under field lore so this reads as "known and
documented," not "the LEDs are broken," the next time it happens at a live game.

## 2026-09-10 (evening, cont.) — a third capture settles n=2, the repaint fix verified, and a magazine-count trap

**A third capture, this time red→blue, full continuous BLE stream:**

```
t=406846..431851  $HIR,4,15,0,0,8,0,0    <- RED (team 0) holding, six clean beacons
t=414481          $TID,1 sent            <- operator switched to blue mid-stream
t=427236          $ALCD 25 -> 24         <- 2 rounds, NO flip
t=431851          $ALCD 23               <- NO flip
t=432381          $ALCD 22               <- NO flip
t=432472          $HIR,4,15,0,1,50,0,0   <- mag 50: flips to team 1 (blue)
t=436846+         $HIR,4,15,0,1,8,0,0    <- blue holding, clean 5.0 s
```

**1. `mag=53` was absent again — n=2 for "53 means the point was NEUTRAL."** The neutral→blue capture carried
both `50` and `53`; both enemy-to-enemy captures (blue→red earlier, and this red→blue) carried only `50`. Two
independent enemy-to-enemy captures, both on continuous streams, neither with a thin-window excuse.
`protocol/brx-ir-protocol.md` upgraded to state n=2.

**2. An OBSERVATION, deliberately not a finding: taking this enemy-held hill cost about 4 magazine
decrements against 1 for the neutral capture earlier** — qualitatively consistent with charge accumulation
(the defender's investment must be exceeded), and a useful lead for rung X. ⚠ **Not a number to trust:** rounds
were fired freely, not counted under control, and **`$ALCD` shows a single `$BUT` press sometimes releasing
TWO rounds** under this gun's fire mode 14 (t=427236 decrements 25→24 across two frames from one press). So
"4 rounds" is really "4 magazine decrements, of unverified magnitude each." Recorded as a qualitative lead, and
as the reason rung X's method note now says: read `$ALCD` deltas and verify magnitude on board A, never trust
one-press-one-round.

**3. The LED repaint fix is CONFIRMED working, not just proposed.** After the `$TID` write plus
`$GLED,,,,5,,,*` (blank) then `$GLED,<c>,<c>,<c>,0,10,,*` and `$HLED,<c>,0,,,10,,*`, the operator confirmed
both gun and headset showed the new team colour after each of two live switches ("yes both red", then "now
they are blue"). F86 and `docs/gotchas.md` updated to say VERIFIED rather than proposed; what remains open is
wiring this sequence into any mode that changes a player's team mid-match — nothing calls it automatically yet.

## 2026-09-10 (evening, cont.) — rung Y answered (negative): fn 28 ignores the `$SIR` sound field

Rung Y in `docs/bench-grenade.md` asked whether a gun can play the beacon itself. Armed via `arm_sequence()`,
`$GSET` t1=1 (polarity gate lifted), AR in slot 0 (`$WEAP` t15=850), grenade set to HILL, gun armed with:

```
$SIR,15,0,U100,28,0,0,1,,*
```

— fn 28 (the no-feedback row F73 already answered) but this time **with a sound id in the `<soundID>`
field**. `U100` is known audible: the operator confirmed it by ear earlier the same evening at the same
`$VOL,80` fired directly via `$PLAY`. Over a run of several beacons, all unambiguously live and registering:

```
$HIR,4,15,0,0,8,0,0   <- repeated, ~5 s apart, no misses
```

**No sound at all.** Operator, verbatim: *"havent heard a tick yet."*

**Finding: fn 28's zero-feedback property (F73) extends to the row's own `<soundID>` — it is not just that
leaving the sound slot empty produces silence, the function itself ignores the field.** Reasoning from the
protocol docs alone predicted a `<soundID>` here would play; it does not, at least not on fn 28.

**Design consequence:** a gun-native beacon cue is not available through fn 28. The alternative rows that DO
give player feedback (fn 8: silent but flashes + vibrates; fn 24-27: a long grenade-ish clip) trade away
exactly the silence that makes fn 28 the row to ship for beacon polling. **All hill audio is therefore
node/phone work** — this settles the question `docs/utility-roadmap.md`'s "Where the hill audio has to live"
section was written to frame, in favour of the phone. Updated there.

⚠ Scope: measured for **fn 28 only**. Whether other protocol-15 functions honour `<soundID>`, and the
`$PSET`-override side effect rung Y also asked about, are both still untested — no sound played at all, so
there was nothing to observe an override on. `docs/bench-grenade.md` rung Y moved from UNMEASURED to ANSWERED
with these two items left open. `docs/FOLLOWUPS.md` F73 extended with this result.

## 2026-09-10 (evening, cont.) — holding a hill does not buff rate of fire (clean null, control inside the measurement)

Same arm as above. Operator believed native KotH grants a rate-of-fire buff (and possibly shield) while
holding the hill. Measured by counting `$ALCD` magazine decrements off the wire, not by feel:

| condition | rounds | duration | ms/round |
|---|---|---|---|
| operator's team HELD the hill | 15 (mag 15->0) | 1530 ms | **102.0** |
| hill ENEMY-held | 31 (mag 31->0) | 3150 ms | **101.6** |

Same gun, same `$WEAP`, same session — only hill ownership differed. **Indistinguishable.**

Stronger than a plain A/B: **the hill flipped to the operator's team PARTWAY THROUGH the second (enemy-held)
burst** — beacons read `team=1` by t=74491 — and the inter-round cadence never changed; the alternating
~90/120 ms pattern runs unbroken across the capture. The control sits inside the measurement itself, not in a
separate trial.

**Conclusion: in a hosted config with fn 28, holding a hill does not change rate of fire.** Consistent with
expectation — fn 28 changes no pools, so no buff was predicted; this confirms rather than discovers. ⚠ **Not**
"native has no RoF buff" — a native game drops the BLE link and cannot be instrumented this way, so a native
buff would be invisible to this method (already recorded in the log). What is measured is that the grenade
does not buff our guns through this row, which is what a hosted mode needs to know.

**Shield:** untested, and expected absent for the same reason (fn 28 grants nothing). The operator's "hill
grants shield" belief needs the hill mapped to a GRANT function (fn 11/18) to test — that is rung D, not this
one; noting it here so it is not read as disproven.

`docs/bench-queue-2026-09-09.md` D7 (which names this exact question) marked answered for the hosted case,
with the native caveat. No new FOLLOWUPS row opened for a settled null.

That is everything from this session's bench work.

---

## 2026-09-11 — King of the Hill, built in one night, and what the reviews found

The bench session (previous entry) proved a grenade hill end to end. This entry is the build that
followed, and it is recorded mainly for the **bugs the process caught**, because every one of them was
found by an instrument or an adversary rather than by anyone reasoning about the code.

**Shipped:** `hillbeacon.py` (the beacon interpreter), `DominationEngine` extended to score possession,
`control.js` (the phone control point: net-difference capture, two-phase conversion, persisted state),
the phone's hill audio (preempt-not-queue, real clip lengths, tick suppression), MC's `koth` mode with a
`station_source` vocabulary and an operator checklist, the possession fact and its recap, and the bench
stage mirroring the grenade half.

**The bugs, in order of how badly they would have bitten:**

1. 🔴 **The first gun in every CLI game could not score, and my own fix caused it.** I added the F69
   wire-0 guard (A5.1: wire 0 is "no identity … never a player"), verified it against a real hill's
   damage word, and wrote up the "fixtures modelled an impossible input" lesson at length. Then
   `driver.py` was found to auto-number the fleet **from 0**, so gun #1 was armed at `$PSET,0` and the
   guard silently dropped its kills: the victim died, nobody scored, no error. **1127 tests passed over
   it**, because every scenario wrote `shooter_id=1` instead of asking the driver what it armed. Same
   shape as the 30 fixtures fixed hours earlier. **A fixture that hardcodes a value the system under test
   also produces cannot see a mismatch between them** — and I wrote that sentence down and then failed to
   apply it one file over.
2. 🔴 **A team with nobody in it could hold a hill, score it, and win.** A grenade still owned by red from
   a previous game accrues from the first beacon in a blue/green match. The silent-adoption path that
   makes "we missed the capture word" work is exactly what hid it. Fixed on the accrual, not the setup —
   removing the `setdefault` would not have worked, because `tick()` creates the accumulator itself.
3. 🔴 **Standing in a hill blocked health regen for a whole match.** The beacon's `$HP` echo reset a 6.0 s
   idle timer every 5.0 s. Nothing damaged the player; the objective's own heartbeat did it.
4. 🔴 **A hill credited its owning team with kills it never made** (the wire-0 guard's original purpose).
5. **The staleness gate compared two clocks** — `seenAt` stamped on raw `Date.now()`, tested against an
   MC-offset clock, so a clock leading by 4 s dropped every advert with no log line. The test could not
   catch it because it stamped `seenAt` with the engine's own clock.
6. **An error message naming the valid values was clipped**: 1395 px of text in a 418 px box, so the
   operator read `…one of: gre`. The "the error is visible" assertion passed the whole time; a geometry
   check caught it. **Visible is not legible.**
7. **`?mock` was teaching the F82 bug** — the in-browser demo drew a KotH roster still half yellow (tid 2)
   while the real path was clean.
8. **The roadmap's economics were wrong by 7x.** It called the grenade a "$30" cheap shortcut to K1; Tony
   paid about **$200**. That inverts the argument the section rested on: the grenade is the *expensive*
   option, and what it buys is the one thing a phone cannot do, which is that you can shoot it.

**The recurring shape, now four instances in two days: a host constant WIDER than an emitter's period
never expires.** `ATTRIB_FUSE_S` 6.0 vs a 5 s beacon; `regen_delay_s` 6.0 vs the same; a 12 s presence
window vs a 2.5 s respawn beacon; a clock offset vs a 4 s gate. It is now a required check on every timer
against every emitter period, and the final review reports which pairs it checked and found correct.

**What the session limit stopped.** Six agents died mid-task at 02:28. Three phone behaviours were
specified and tested but not implemented (**F101**, tests skipped with a reason rather than deleted), the
stage never got a station-path mirror (**F102**), and the phone control point has no match lifecycle
(**F103**) — which is F70's grenade-persistence trap rebuilt one layer over, complete with a `SETUP:`
warning that still says power-cycle a *grenade*.
> ⚠ **CORRECTION, 2026-09-11 (same day): the F103 sentence above is WRONG and is withdrawn.** The lifecycle
> reset was implemented before that agent's limit hit — `utility.js`'s `applyStationConfig` keeps `wasGame`
> and calls `resetPoint()` when the armed `game` changes, which clears owner, progress, `holdMs`, the capture
> log and `refusedSeen`. It was filed off a reviewer's report of an older tree and relayed without anyone
> opening the file, which is the same failure mode this entry spends three paragraphs warning about. F103 is
> retitled to the two items that ARE real (possession clamped by `MAX_STEP_MS`; `refusedSeen` sticky within a
> match) plus a third found while checking: `STATION_SOURCES` has no phone value, so a phone-sourced KotH
> cannot be configured through MC at all — which is why the `SETUP:` half of the claim looked true.
> **F101 and F102 are unaffected and stand as written.**

**Tomorrow's bench is `docs/bench-critical-2026-09-11.md`**, six readings in three setups, nothing blocked
on code. Its sharpest item is one nobody had written down: **B2, whether a gun moved off protocol 0 can
still capture a hill at all.** Every capture ever measured was a protocol-0 word, so F91's fix for the
hill chipping attackers might buy that immunity by deleting shoot-to-capture — the very mode it protects.

## 2026-09-11 (late) — the followups triage, and what a keyboard could close

**Ask (Tony):** the followups file had grown past 150 ids; triage it fully (category, priority, dependencies,
who can close it), then work the critical bugs and core platform gaps. No hardware in this session.

**The triage is a new file, `followups-triage.md` (retired 2026-09-12, see FOLLOWUPS §0)** — an index over `FOLLOWUPS.md`
(the rows stay the truth). Every open id sorted into: bug in active code (30, 24 keyboard-only) · core platform
gap (18, 14 keyboard) · bench-gated (46) · decision (14) · future feature (29) · hygiene (21), each with its
blocker and what it unblocks, plus a dependency spine. The headline: of nine 🔴 ids only THREE were keyboard
work (F104, E1, E5); the rest are bench or lessons. Then the keyboard list was worked top down.

**Closed (18, plus one opened and closed):** F81 · F34 · F97 · F47 · F55 · F53 · F41 · F90 · F101 · F103 ·
**F104** · F105 · F86 · F77 · F64 · F31 · F84 · F92 · F94. Narrowed: F80, S5, F13. One line each is in
`archive/followups-closed.md` under *Closed 2026-09-11 (late)*. Five commits on `claude/worktree-followups-triage-jz55vy`.

**What the code work found, in the order it would have bitten:**

1. 🔴 **No station has ever been armed, and the reason was on BOTH ends of the wire (F104, then F105).** MC
   had nothing that sent `station_config` — that was known. What was not known: the phone's own kind whitelist
   (`app/src/transport/envelope.js` `MC_KINDS`) did not list it either, so a server-only fix would have been
   dropped as a malformed frame before `utility.js` saw it. And the same list **never had `alert`** — so every
   A11.4 alert MC has ever sent (flag taken, the infected broadcast, last survivor) died at the phone's
   `decode()`, counted as `malformed`, since alerts shipped. The engine suite could not see it: every test calls
   `onMcMessage` directly. `test_mc_stations.py` now pins the two lists equal, which is the guard the
   2026-09-07 MC-side omission asked for and did not get.
2. **Two of F101's three "unimplemented" behaviours were implemented.** The previous session's tests revived a
   dead engine with a bare `$HP,45` (not a revive) and ended a match with a kind named `end` (the wire is
   `control{cmd:end}`). Un-skipped with the right stimuli, both passed on the existing code; only (b) needed
   F103's `phone` value. Same lesson as the F103 headline that was withdrawn this morning: a test that fails is
   evidence about the TEST first.
3. **The possession tally under-reported by design of one shared variable (F103).** `control.js` added the
   1 s-clamped step meant for capture work to `holdMs`, so a throttled station credited its owner one second per
   tick however long it held the point. Two clocks now.
4. **A four-team hill was refused only by accident (F97)** — the F82 tid-2 check caught it with advice a fourth
   single-member team cannot follow. Refused by count now, at PUT, in `validate()` and in the engine.
5. **An infected gun was painted back to the human colour 2.5 s after every revive (F86, phone path).**
   `gun.take` is compiled for the ARMING team and `_gunTake` used it for life. The bundle carries
   `team_flip_take` per tid now; the CLI driver blanks then paints after `$TID` (the sequence Tony verified).
6. **The Designer let the operator step into a 400 (F34)**: 1–2 s respawn was refused by the server and the
   control offered it anyway; and the node itself had no floor, so a config from any other path spawned inside
   the F13 wedge.
7. **The MC console's ITEMS panel overflowed its own card** — found only in Chromium against `?mock`, not by
   the 111 vitest cases: a five-way segmented control in a 320 px card pushed CONTROL POINT outside the
   clip-path, where it could not be clicked. `Seg` gained `wrap`. The unit suite renders text; it does not
   measure boxes.

**Method notes worth keeping.** (a) Research ran as three read-only agents in parallel before any edit; every
"still open?" question was answered from the tree, which is how F31 turned out mostly fixed and F34 half fixed.
(b) Every fix carries a paired CONTROL assertion in the house style. (c) `/polish-loop` ran on the PR afterwards (three
parallel reviewers, then fixes): one High -- a station assigned or cleared while the match was ARMED or LIVE re-pushed
`config` to every HUD, whose `_applyConfig` rewrites the head and clears `spawned`, silencing every hit handler for
the rest of the match; refused now in the operator's voice, and the re-push is LOBBY-only. Mediums: the readout's
revert-to-rest still painted the arming team after an infection flip (F86's other half); evicting an assigned
station left its id in every allow-list; a station could be put on a tid no team was on; the ITEMS card had no
re-arm path when the phone disagreed and dropped a draft on the first heartbeat; a Playwright gate pinned two
source chips. Lows are one row, F106.

**Not done, deliberately:** E1/E2 (extensibility) — the next keyboard rung after F102; F68's heartbeat repaint
(only bites when S17 ships and wants an eye); persisting station assignments across an MC restart (S5 leftover).
**Nothing here was run on hardware**; the bench sheet is unchanged.

## 2026-09-11 (afternoon) — the site refactor: two doors, one source, and a landing that cannot go stale

**Ask (Tony):** "I want two different things": a BRX manual that is the community's single source of truth,
dry and searchable, AND the Open BRX platform marketed "like an Apple product" (Mission Control, HUD, brx-mcp,
with screenshots, animation, a11y, SEO, responsive). The first site attempt went full marketing, the second full
docs. Also: maintainability is critical, repo markdown is the only truth, nothing on the site may drift from
the docs. Root = marketing for the whole ecosystem (Tony's call), manual one click away. No hardware.

**Process.** Design canvas first (three artboards in Mission Control's armory family; Tony picked
"The BRX, unlocked." over four other headlines), then the build. Two sonnet lanes in parallel (content
migration, screenshot pipeline), generator + CSS + motion + gate in the main session, then two adversarial
reviewers (browser-breaker, stranger/fact-checker).

**What exists now.** `docs/platform/{index,docs,install,run,modes,leds,download}.md` → `/`, `/docs/*`,
`/download`; `docs/manual/index.md` is the manual's own landing at `/manual/`; the six manual pages are
unchanged. `site/build.mjs` has two templates: `doc` (unchanged column, light/dark) and `landing`
(`site/lib/landing.mjs`: plain CommonMark read by SHAPE, no DSL — `##` section, `###` headline, image-only
paragraph = shots row, `**Bold.**` list = captions, link list = buttons, bare fence = terminal, ```data fence =
component). Components render from repo source only (`site/lib/facts.mjs`: modes off `state.py` MODES, weapon
classes off `weapons.json` + `tokens.ts` ROLE, the release off the APK sidecar, the six manual doors off the
page map with each page's first sentence). **Landing pages carry capabilities, never status:** the build FAILS
on a date, a version number, "not yet"/"unfinished"/"coming soon" or a match report in either landing.
**Screenshots are generated** (`site/shots.mjs`: MC `?mock` + HUD `?demo` through Playwright, 10 JPEGs,
`manifest.json` with the tree hashes of `webapp/mc/src` and `app/src`; `mcp/tests/test_site_shots.py` fails
when either tree moves past the manifest). Fonts self-hosted (Oswald variable + Chakra Petch 500/600/700 latin,
`site/public/fonts/`). Motion: IntersectionObserver reveals, a one-shot count-up, a pinned Mission Control shot
with scrolling captions, a terminal type-out, a 0.6x parallax on the grenade backdrop; all off under
`prefers-reduced-motion` and everything visible with JS off. SEO: per-page title/description, canonical,
Open Graph, JSON-LD SoftwareApplication on `/`, sitemap, llms.txt. The gate grew from 18 to ~42 steps per
width (12–12g: loaded images, numbers equal repo data, every button resolves, reveal + reduced-motion, landmarks,
heading order, 4.5:1 lede contrast, 44 px targets, self-hosted fonts, hero search, placeholder honesty).

**What the build and the gate caught, in order:** (1) `webapp/download/` was protected as a whole, so the
`/download` page could not be written — protection is now per path (`build.json` and any `.apk`), and the
generated `download/index.html` is git-ignored. (2) The landing counted 2,634 sounds: `buildSounds` includes the
157 app-only ids; the strip now counts `on_gun` only (2,477, matching the manual). (3) The hero lede rendered as
body copy because the H1 token made the hero "non-empty" — a lede flag per target fixed it. (4) The pinned MC
layout placed the lede in the heading's grid area and the two overlapped. (5) Door blurbs were the first SOURCE
LINE of a hard-wrapped paragraph ("...and the Callsign app. It"); now the whole paragraph, cut at the first
sentence, 160 chars at a word boundary. (6) Lazy images below the fold read `naturalWidth 0` until the gate
scrolled like a reader. (7) Two test-side bugs: the mode-count regex also matched the `lan` dict's
`{"mode": ...}`, and the section anchor sits on the `<h2>`, not the `<section>`. (8) On the phone,
`scrollIntoViewIfNeeded` on a section taller than the screen centres it and leaves its first line above the
viewport, so the reveal never fired — scroll the element, not the section. (9) `hud-down.jpg` at the specified
28.6 s showed a player alive at 36 hp; the demo dies at ~29.2 s; capture moved to 29.6 s (a real DOWN frame,
verified by engine state `alive:false` and by eye).

**Numbers.** Gate: 82 steps green at 1280 and 390 (one transient network failure on the external-link step,
green on rerun). `python3 mcp/run_tests.py`: 1169 passed, 0 failed, 47 skipped (system python).
`docs/manual/README.md`, `docs/site/FORMAT.md` (new "Landing pages" section), `CLAUDE.md`, root and app READMEs
updated. Old `/platform/*` URLs redirect to `/` and `/docs/*`; `/manual/` is a live page again.

**What the reviewers found (acted on):** the fact-checker caught the terminal transcript on `/` and the install
page crediting `identify` with firmware, battery and headset state (`identify` returns reachability and
generation only; `diagnose` reads firmware and battery, and NO command reports headset link); the arsenal
copy advertising recoil that no shipped weapon enables (t21/t22 stock 100/100, S17 open behind F68) — cut,
replaced with the per-weapon definition that IS written; "ammo" stations described as being built when
`spec/utility.md` §5 lists them as designed only — reworded to the phone respawn station that exists; the
mode briefs rendering "the score cap: or" because `facts.mjs` swapped the briefs' em-dash asides for colons
(comma now); "MCP" never expanded for a non-developer (one clause added); door blurbs cut mid-clause with a
bare ellipsis (now cut at the last clause boundary as a sentence); and FOLLOWUPS §1 still calling the repo
private. Left as Tony's call: the hero lede's "writes the match / rides each gun" metaphors and "Muster" in
the MC headline, both approved copy. The browser-breaker (15 pages x 4 viewports, every control clicked) found
four: (1) **in-page anchors landed the heading 4-8 px under the sticky header** because the browser scrolls to
a heading where it IS, and a heading still translated 18 px down by its reveal then slides up under the
header (headings now fade in place, and a landing h2 clears the header by 40 px); (2) **with JavaScript off
the whole landing below the h1 was invisible**: the hidden-by-default reveal rules had no gate (now scoped to
`html.js`, a class set by an inline script before first paint); (3) the burger and theme buttons were 38 px on
a phone (44 now); (4) the skip link scrolled but did not move focus (`main` has `tabindex=-1`). Each is a gate
step now. What held up: zero console errors, failed requests, overflow, clipped headings or missing alt
across 60 page-viewport pairs; contrast 5.4:1 to 13.3:1 everywhere sampled; theme choice persists on doc
pages while both landings stay dark.

**Visual QA pass (Tony: "test it like an Apple QA would", 1920x1080 and 390x844, every screen captured and
read).** Five defects the gate had not caught: (1) the hero photo never appeared with motion on: the no-JS fix
raised the hidden rule to `html.js .hero .photo` and the `.in` rule lost on specificity; (2) the pinned Mission
Control shot stuck into the two-shot row beneath it, because `position: sticky` is bounded by the containing
block, the whole section, not the grid area; the shot now sits in its own cell; (3) the small terminal in the
"Get it" card was blank forever: every `.term .t-l` waited for a reveal that only terminals with `data-reveal`
get; (4) at 1080 tall the hero photo sat below the fold, so the hero lost 44 px of top air; (5) on a phone
"DEVELOPER" broke mid-word in a manual door and "BRX-MCP" wrapped in the card title. The gate's normal-motion
check now scrolls the whole page and asserts NOTHING is left under opacity 1, photo and terminal lines
included. **Navigation (Tony: "I get lost; the nav must say if you are in BRX world or Platform world").**
Tony then replaced the segmented switch with his own design: the WORDMARK names the place, with the HUD's blue
slash (`BRX/` + COMBAT HUD is the phone's): `OPEN-BRX/` on the ecosystem landing, `PLATFORM/` and
`PLATFORM/ DOCS` in the platform half, `BRX/` and `BRX/ DOCS` in the manual; clicking it opens the core places
(Open BRX, Platform, Download, BRX manual, GitHub) as a keyboard-navigable menu. Gate 1b asserts the wordmark
per page; 12 opens the menu, checks its places and closes it with Escape back to the button.

**Second QA round (Tony, by eye on his monitor):** the header call to action was underlined (a bare `a` inside
`.top`); the chamfer `clip-path` was clipping every focus ring, so buttons, doors and the hero search now draw
focus INSIDE the shape and the gate asserts a negative outline offset; the counts strip touched the section
below it; the pinned Mission Control section ran the full 1920 width with left-flush text while everything
else sat in 1312 px (constrained now); the hero phone screen looked composited, so the REAL screen from Tony's
photo (glass, reflections, smudges) is perspective-warped into the edited phone instead of a flat render; the
HUD screenshots were soft on a large monitor (`shots.mjs` captures at device scale 2 now, the size cap moved to
450 KB, phone shots are classed by aspect ratio rather than by pixel width); the grenade backdrop overlaid copy
on the grenade at tablet widths (below 900 px the photo is a plain figure above the copy; wider, the copy is
capped at 52% and the photo anchors 72%/62%); and INF, LMS and EXT have engines but no real match, so
`state.py` MODES carries a `proven` flag and the tile badges "In development" off it (the gate's tiny-text
sweep immediately caught the badge at 10 px).

**Suite audit (third reviewer lens, per `ui-build-verify` §5).** Real false-pass risks found and closed: the
landing asserted `>= 8` sections and images against 9 in the source (one could vanish); nothing tied a rendered
screenshot to WHICH shot it should be (exact identity in source order now); the sounds counter was never
checked against data and only the first count-up was watched (all three now); no geometry check on the pinned
layout (lede/shot/captions bounding boxes now); door blurbs were never read (must end as a sentence); the
redirects proved "lands somewhere live", not "lands on the right page" (the four moved pages are pinned);
the theme toggle's absence on landings, the landing's own burger, the eyebrow numbering, the 404 page's links
and the LaserTagMods credit line were asserted nowhere (all are now). The eyebrow check itself then caught
the download cards reusing the eyebrow class: scoped to section heads.

**Third round (Tony):** the docs overview read `PLATFORM/` with no DOCS label although its links sit on the
left like every doc page, so the rule is now "doc layout carries DOCS, a marketing landing is bare", with a
hairline after the wordmark on doc pages; the wordmark's icon and text were on different centre lines
(baseline alignment; centred now, asserted within 3 px); the primary button's hover turned its label light
blue on light blue (a generic `main.landing a:hover`; asserted 4.5:1 on hover now); the outline button's
border stopped at the chamfer's diagonal (a CSS border cannot follow a clip-path, so the edge is a clipped
`::before` layer under an inset fill); Download left the places menu; and a third landing was born,
`docs/platform/platform.md` at `/platform/`, marketing the software as a match runs (kit up, write the game,
arm and start, play, recap, brx-mcp, get it) with "Get the app" as its header button. Landing headers now
derive their section links from their own `##` headings. `/platform/` is a live page again, so its redirect
rule is gone. Gate 12h covers the new landing. **Vocabulary (Tony):** "tagger", never "gun", across every platform page and
the manual landing (98 swaps, word-bounded so a shotgun stays a shotgun); the manual's hardware pages keep
their own terms. The point is search: the site should not rank for gun queries.

**Hero, final call for today (Tony: "that hero image is still bad"):** the Gemini photo is out. The hero is a
product shot of the two REAL screens, the Mission Control board large with the phone HUD overlapping its lower
left corner (`hero-duo` in `landing.mjs`: a hero image paragraph with two shots), nothing generated. Swapping
a photo back in is one line of markdown. Tony may reshoot and re-prompt later; the real-screen warp stays the
method for any photo that shows the phone.

**Hero, second reversal:** Tony's next Gemini render was good enough, so the photo is back: real screen warped
in from the original, the invented logo cloned off, the watermark corner cropped away. A photo shown AFTER a
section's copy now renders whole (only a photo that opens a section is a backdrop), which gave the landing a
"The kit" section with the cleaned flat lay (same treatment) and a real `og.jpg` share card off it.

**Hero, settled:** Tony's third Gemini render, prompted with the screen declared off-limits, kept the real HUD
legible, so the phone is untouched this time (Tony: "leave the phone alone"); only the invented emblem on the
receiver is cloned off and the frame cropped to the slot. The kit photo keeps Gemini's phone too, same rule.

**Newcomers and search (Tony):** every marketing landing gained a "What is this?" section right after the hero:
what a BRX is (a rifle-shaped laser tag tagger and sensor headset by Battle Company, linked), what Open BRX
does with it, in plain words, with Battle Company's own "serious system for the home user, not a toy" framing.
Kept out of the header nav (a heading ending in "?" is never a nav link). Headlines trimmed: the platform to

**The HUD on the landing is LIVE (Tony's ask):** the arsenal section's phone frame is the real Companion build
(`app/www`, shipped at `/demo/hud/`, `?demo&kit`: a real engine with a fake tagger and a fake Mission Control),

Vocabulary note: the newcomer sections say "laser tag gun" (Tony: "laser tag tagger is trying too hard");

**Deploy failure, self-inflicted:** the first push with the HUD demo never went live. Workers Builds reported

## 2026-09-11 (late, second session) — the second triage pass: what a keyboard closed while the hardware is in the post

**Method.** The triage of the morning (`followups-triage.md`) still stood, so this pass re-read every row against
the tree, corrected two (F36 named APK 0.1.7; F99's firmware exists since the M5StickS3 commit), and worked the
keyboard list in four parallel lanes on disjoint files — the stage mirror, the wire contracts, the phone engine +
fake, the station leftovers — then a three-lens polish loop over the whole diff (server / engine+stage / UI+docs),
two rounds. Nothing here has run on hardware.

**Closed:** F102 · F54 · S11 · F57 · F15 · F78 · E1 · S5 (one dated line each in `archive/followups-closed.md`).
**Narrowed:** F58 (b) · Q18 (the print half) · S10 (the role contract, A19) · F106 ((a)-(e), (i)). **Opened:** F107, the
loop's Lows. **Amendments:** A18 mode_params · A19 roles · A20 stun.

**What the loop found that the lanes had missed** (all fixed, with controls):
1. **A stun before the first shot of a new life restored the LAST life's reserve** (engine.js and the stage alike:
   the spawn reset cleared the mag map and not the reserve map). Silent wrong gun state; both maps reset now.
2. **The late-fact recap re-store dropped the stations rows.** `_restore_recap` called `scorer.recap()` bare, so the
   first fact after END — the outbox flush, every real match — rewrote `last_recap` and the DB row without them.
   One `_scorer_recap()` helper for all three call sites.
3. `stun` was not in `_CONFIG_KEYS`, so a PUT dropped it in silence and no game could stun over MC (the F40 shape:
   the whitelist ignores unknown keys rather than refusing them). And the stage's `set_profile` allow-list lacked
   it too — found by the browser drive, the page's selector changed nothing while RE-ARM lit.
4. A pre-A18 session.json restored a koth config with no `mode_params`, `_validate` skipped the ABSENT set, and the
   wire pushed without it — against A18's complete-or-absent. Normalised on restore.
5. The stage's background boot could tear down a link the operator had made meanwhile (a silent gun swap). Guarded.
6. F78's gate made an existing F69 test vacuous: `test_fake_game` beaconed at a fresh fake with no `<15,0>` row, so
   the driver never saw a beacon and "the hill scores for nobody" passed for the wrong reason. Armed now.
7. A player phone re-helloing as a utility kept `ready` and its ack, so kit→lobby could advance on a phone that had
   become a station. Unbound like an evict now.
8. `screens.mjs` #45 had been failing on main since F34: the demo's scanner fixture said 1 s while the engine floors
   respawn at 3 s, so the harness read HOLD where it expected the trigger prompt. The fixture says 3 s now.
9. **(round 2) A STATUS carrying `role: utility` from a BOUND player node re-typed it as a station without
   unbinding it**, so END and PANIC skipped that HUD and `_finish` pulled no log from it. A status never changes
   what a bound node is (A8); only the utility hello does. The round-1 CONTROL had enshrined the half-flipped state.
10. **(round 2)** the saved-game "PLAYING" signature compared the live config with `vip_player_id` in it, which a
    saved game never carries, so a game with a VIP never matched its preset; the WITHHELD role line and the stage's
    boot guard had no tests.

**Numbers at close:** mcp 1275 pass (90 files) · app 269 pass · webapp/mc 127 pass, tsc clean · screens.mjs 184/184 ·
stage suites 99 · docs hygiene 8.

**Not done, deliberately:** E2 (the other three touch points), E5 (phone audio: needs ears), B19 (needs a gun to
validate), F88 multi-point on phones, S3 extraction on phones (needs S5's zone = a station beacon; the plumbing now
exists). The HANDOFF sits at its 150-line limit and was rewritten, not appended.

## 2026-09-11 (late, third session) — dogfooding "start Mission Control" as a fresh agent, and what the docs got wrong

**Method.** Follow only the doc trail (CLAUDE.md → docs/README.md → the MC READMEs → the field/mac runbooks) and
try to get a Mission Control board on screen in WSL, noting every stumble. No hardware.

**What worked.** `cd mcp && ../.venv/bin/python -m brx_mcp.mc --demo --fake-net --no-auth --ephemeral` boots in ~2 s
from the WSL venv with no extra installs; `/` serves the built console; `/api/state` reports `lobby`, 8 nodes; headless
Chromium (Playwright from `app/node_modules`) renders the LOBBY with GUN-A…H, 8/8 READY, zero console errors.

**Stumbles, in the order a newcomer meets them.**
1. **No page answered "run MC on the dev box".** The answer was split across five files (CLAUDE.md one-liner,
   `mc/README.md` flags, `webapp/mc/README.md` dev server, `field-runbook-mc.md` Mac install, `mac-dev-runbook.md`
   restart/hard-reload), and `docs/README.md`'s "You are…" table had no row for it. Which Python runs MC in WSL was
   nowhere: CLAUDE.md says gun work needs Windows Python, and MC touches no gun, but nobody said the WSL venv is the one.
2. **`mc/README.md` and `field-runbook-mc.md` both said `--fake-net` = "8 simulated nodes / demo nodes".** The code
   (`__main__.py build()`) seeds nodes only with `--demo` AND `--fake-net`; `--fake-net` alone is an empty MUSTER,
   `--demo` alone starts the REAL node server. Both files now say so.
3. **The banner prints before the bind.** A demo MC from 08:46 still owned :8765; my launch printed `Mission Control
   http://…:8765/` then `[Errno 98]`, and my first curl was answered by the leftover — I nearly reported its LOBBY as
   mine. The e2e (`koth.mjs`) already refuses a squatter; the human/agent path had no warning. → **F108**.
4. `nodes: ws://0.0.0.0:0/ws` under `--fake-net` reads as a bug; it is the fake transport's placeholder.
5. `webapp/mc/dist` is git-ignored and was 10 source files stale here; MC serves it without complaint. The
   restart-vs-hard-reload rule lived only in the MAC runbook.
6. A Playwright script in the scratchpad fails to import `playwright` (ESM ignores NODE_PATH); it must sit under `app/`
   or `site/`. `webapp/mc/README.md` says "already installed", not where the script has to live.
7. Minor: the field runbook said "add --host 0.0.0.0 to bind all interfaces" — that is already the default.

**Writes.** `mcp/brx_mcp/mc/README.md` is now the single "Start it" page (command, expected output, flag table as the
code reads it, the busy-port check, served vs dev UI, where the rest lives); `docs/README.md` gained the row and the
build-section link; CLAUDE.md's layout line carries the verified one-liner and the two traps; the field runbook's two
wrong lines are fixed; F108 opened; HANDOFF gained one where-things-live row (sits at exactly 150 lines).

## 2026-09-11 (bench) — F69 REFUTED: the hill's "chip damage" is manufactured INSIDE the gun by fn 24, not a word in the air

One gun (`Tactix-3D4F`) on BLE, board A (receiver) on COM7 facing the grenade, board B (emitter) on COM8, grenade in
HILL mode NEUTRAL 3-6 ft from the gun, friendly fire ON, gun on team 1, arms generated by `mcp/tools/armgen.py`.

**Method lesson first, because it ate the first ten minutes and nearly produced a false F74: `ir-emit` returns to
the shell long before the board stops transmitting.** `ir-emit <bits> COM8 1000` handed control back immediately,
but board A still saw the same word in the air at ~150 ms/frame for close to two minutes (still live at 15:35:57,
quiet by 15:36:14). Six mag-20 words at that rate killed a gun in 0.4 s — `rapid_fire.py`'s mag-1 default exists
for exactly this reason, and the one-off command used tonight skipped it. A re-spawn fired while the emitter was
still running died again in 90 ms and was nearly written up as a fresh F74 latch; board A's still-live capture is
what caught it. The fix (a `--wait` flag on the emit tool, plus a `gotchas.md` line) landed elsewhere this session.
**Bench-side lesson to keep regardless of the tooling fix: don't trust "quiet" until the receiver confirms it.**

**Arm A — the stock table, no proto-15 row, hill beside it ~110 s: nothing, and the null proves nothing.** Board A
was facing the GUN in this window, not the grenade, so "no `$HIR` of any kind" is recorded for completeness only —
geometry was uncontrolled.

**Arm B — stock table + `$SIR,15,0,,24,0,0,1,,*` (fn 24, the row `bench-grenade.md` shipped before F73 moved ship
guidance to fn 28): a neutral, unshot hill killed a 45 HP / 70 armour gun in ~75 s.** Every ~5.0 s the beacon
landed cleanly (`$HIR,4,15,0,2,8,0,0` / `$HIR,0,15,0,2,8,0,0`, sensors 4 and 0, no pool change), and roughly 4 s
after each beacon the gun logged `$HIR,0,0,0,2,8,0,0` one to three times, ~420 ms apart, each taking 8 armour.
Tony heard the same sequence every cycle — hiss, explosion, armour-hit sound — and a new beacon cut the clip short
and restarted it. **Board A, pointed at the grenade across the same two windows (capB1, capB2), recorded 13 and 8
beacons — `proto=15 team=2 mag=8`, and ZERO protocol-0 words.** Whatever the gun is reporting as damage is not on
the wire.

⭐ **So the "chip damage" is manufactured INSIDE THE GUN by fn 24: it registers the beacon, plays its hiss +
explosion clip, and about 4 s later applies 1-3 blast ticks of the beacon's own magnitude, reported back as
ordinary `$HIR` protocol-0 hits carrying the beacon's team.** This is a function property, not a wire event. The
protocol doc already noted fn 24 "gives a flash, a buzz and a long grenade-ish clip on every beacon" (F73,
2026-09-10) but stopped at the clip; the clip is followed by real, delayed damage that a receiver watching only
the air will never see, because there is nothing in the air to see.

**Replay test: no self-replay reproduced tonight.** Re-armed Arm B at 200 armour / 100 HP: four cycles of ticks
(200 → 104) tracked the beacon exactly, the last tick pair landing ~5 s after the last beacon; the grenade then
went to HILL OFF and the gun sat for 60+ s with **nothing further** — no more ticks, and board A recorded zero
protocol-0 words in the same window. Every tick traces to a live beacon and stops when the beacon stops.

**Arm C — the SHIPPED row, `$SIR,15,0,,28,0,0,1,,*` (fn 28): a live neutral hill chips nobody.** 200 armour /
100 HP, board A facing the grenade, 70 s: 14 clean beacons landed, pools held 100/200 throughout, zero protocol-0
`$HIR` on the gun and zero protocol-0 words on board A. **With the row we ship, standing beside an unattended hill
costs nothing.**

**Also recorded, not chased:** the first word out of the grenade after every power-on decodes `proto=15 team=0
mag=55` on both instruments — a boot word, filed for the record rather than interpreted.

**F69, refuted as originally framed.** Its ~106 s death (2026-09-10, early session) was measured with exactly
this same row on the cell: that entry's own log reads "Adding `$SIR,15,0,,24,0,0,1,,*` made beacons appear
immediately" two paragraphs before the ~106 s death is reported. The two findings agree rather than conflict — it
was always fn 24, never an ambient `proto=0` wire word. **F91 is retired with it:** there is no wire-level damage
word to dodge by moving weapons off protocol 0, so the tradeoff of going deaf to native guns (bench-critical B2,
never run) does not need paying. `docs/bench-critical-2026-09-11.md` rungs A1, B1 and B2 are answered/moot; A2,
B3, C1 and C2 are unaffected and stand.

**F74 downgraded, not closed.** The trigger for the 2026-09-10 latch is now understood as this same fn 24-27
blast family — a delayed, self-timed tick that reads exactly like a stuck IR event in the gun's own log. But
tonight's replay test held pools steady for 60+ s with the beacon off; **self-replay with NO IR in the air at
all, the original 2026-09-10 observation, was not reproduced.** That stays open and unexplained. The rule to ship
is simple regardless of the mechanism — never put fn 24-27 on any cell — so the node-side guard F74 proposed is
no longer justified by tonight's evidence. Downgraded 🔴 → 🟡.

**Writes.** `docs/FOLLOWUPS.md`: F69 and F91 struck to `archive/followups-closed.md`; F74 rewritten in place and
downgraded; F70's cross-ref to F91 updated. `docs/bench-critical-2026-09-11.md`: A1/B1/B2 marked ANSWERED with a
pointer here. `protocol/brx-ir-protocol.md` and `protocol/brx-protocol.md` gain fn 24's blast-tick behaviour in
the function map, corrected from "registers, no pool change".

## 2026-09-11 (bench, second session) — F23 CLOSED: fn 36/37 only scale on the HEADSET, and the scale is the compiled crit_modifier

One gun (Tactix-3D4F) over BLE, one ESP32 emitter (board B) on COM8 at 3 ft, words from
`brx_mcp.irbridge.encode_word(player=42, team=2, damage=9, proto=0, subtype=<0|1|3>)`, victim team 1, armour 200
via `$PSET`, the stock 10-row `$SIR` table, arms from `mcp/tools/armgen.py` (`1 5 ar armor=200 crit_modifier=<m>`).
Five words per set, one per second; every word's `$HIR` sensor field and `$HP` delta recorded.

| `$GSET` t7 (crit modifier) | sensor | fn 1 (sub 0) | fn 36 (sub 1) | fn 37 (sub 3) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 100 | headset dome (sensor 0) | 9 | 13 | 27 |
| 100 | gun body (sensor 4) | 9 | 9 | 9 |
| 50 | headset dome | (not run) | 11 | 18 |
| 0 | headset dome | (not run) | 9 | 9 |
| 100 (closing control) | headset dome | | | 27 |

Every set landed 5/5 except fn 37 at modifier 50 (4/5; one word did not register — not chased). `$HIR` token 6
(the crit bit) was 0 on every one of the 15 headset hits; token 7 echoed the fired subtype (0/1/3) so each row is
confirmed per hit. Base magnitude 9 held throughout.

⭐ **Formula (floored).** Body sensor: applied = magnitude, for all three functions — fn 36/37 included. Headset
sensor: fn 1 = magnitude (unscaled); **fn 36 = magnitude × (1 + t7/200)**; **fn 37 = magnitude × (1 + 2·t7/100)**.
At the MC-compiled default t7 = 50 that is ×1.25 and ×2 exactly.

**Reconciliation.** This does not overturn either of the two earlier readings — it explains why they disagreed.
The 2026-08-27 "×1.0 matrix" was rig-pinned to the gun body the whole time (correct: body is always ×1). The
2026-09-02 reading (×1.25 on fn 36, ×2 on fn 37) was taken on the headset at the MC-compiled t7=50 (also
correct). Neither run recorded which sensor caught the hit, so for two weeks two correct measurements looked like
a contradiction. F23's own opening evidence — an 18/hit Callsign capture on headset sensor 0 versus 9/hit on gun
body 4, same weapon, same life — is the same mechanism, read cold, before anyone knew t7 was the lever.

**The closing control.** The 100→50→0→100 walk on fn 37 at the headset (row 5 of the table) is what proves t7,
not the sensor or the function alone, drives the scale: t7=0 read fn 37 back to a plain ×1 on the headset (same
row as fn 1's control), and returning to t7=100 brought it straight back to ×2 (27, matching row 1's 27 within
the same base magnitude). If the function alone gated it, t7=0 would have changed nothing.

**Also measured tonight, same gun — F27 sub-heading, reload timing.** `$WEAP` t18 = 1400 ms (assault rifle) →
handle press (`$BUT,2,1`) to `$ALCD` refill measured **1686 / 1701 / 1701 ms** across three pulls. A pull on a
full magazine does nothing. Burst rifle (`$WEAP` t18 = 1700 ms, catalog `reload_ms` 1700) measured **2160 / 2160
/ 2107 ms** across three pulls. Charge rifle (`$WEAP` t18 = 2500 ms) measured **3220 / 3166 / 3234 ms** across
three pulls, vs catalog `reload_ms` 2500; it reloads by handle exactly like the other two (button 2). Two extra
observations on the charge rifle, worth one line each: **(1)** the reload clears accumulated heat to 0 — this
weapon overheats, `$ALCD`'s heat token climbed past 100 and "overheating" latched true under sustained fire,
then reset to 0 on reload; **(2)** it spawned with mag 100 despite `$AMMO,0,20` in the arm, because the weapon's
own maxClip (t16=100) governs, not the arm command. Tony confirmed by ear the charge rifle has distinct/unique
reload sounds. **All three weapons measured tonight refill SLOWER than the catalog**: assault rifle
1686/1701/1701 vs 1400 (about +290 ms), burst rifle 2160/2160/2107 vs 1700 (about +440 ms), charge rifle
3220/3166/3234 vs 2500 (about +700 ms). **Consequence: the RELOADING HUD takeover runs for the catalog
`reload_ms`, so on all three it currently ends before the gun has actually refilled.** F27 now has all three
primary-class weapons measured; sidearms and melee are still open, so F27 stays open for those.

**Method lesson.** Record the `$HIR` sensor field on every hit. Two correct readings looked like a contradiction
for two weeks because neither did.

**Writes.** `docs/FOLLOWUPS.md`: F23 closed and struck to `archive/followups-closed.md` (removed from the 🟠
lane index and the bench-queue register bullet at §9); F62 gains a warning that its "9-vs-18" expectation is
this fn-37 effect and the probe must run at t7=0 or on the body to isolate t6; F71 gains a cross-ref noting its
t12 `extraHeadsetDamage` candidate is a different, still-open mechanism. `mcp/brx_mcp/mc/compile.py`:
`_SIR_MULTIPLIER` replaced by `headset_multiplier(fn, crit_modifier)`; `damage()`/`hits_to_kill()`/`time_to_kill()`
docstrings and the `_SIR_NO_POOL`-block comment now say plainly that they compute the GUN-BODY (×1),
guaranteed-kill number; `validate()`'s multiplier warning now names the headset multiplier at the game's actual
compiled crit_modifier instead of a flat constant. `mcp/tests/test_headset_multiplier.py` (new) pins the three
measured points (t7=50 on fn 36/37, t7=0 as unscaled) plus every non-multiplier function at 1.0.
`docs/weapon-design.md` (banner, §6.2 balance table + narrative + shipped-row table + the crit-formula pseudocode
+ the "Crit is a live mechanic" note), `protocol/brx-protocol.md` (§`$GSET` token 7, the function map's two
multiplier rows and its lead-in caveat), `protocol/callsign-extract/protocol-classes.md` (`t5`/applied-damage
note) and `docs/reference/weapons.md` (both `dmg` column notes) all corrected off the flat ×1.25/×2/"1.5-if-crit"
reading to the sensor-gated, t7-dependent one. `mcp/tools/bench_common.py`'s stale "DISPUTED multiplier pair"
comment updated to point at `headset_multiplier()`.

## 2026-09-11 (evening, bench) -- 🔊 the sound pass: $PLAY has an INTERRUPT slot and a QUEUE slot, and 90 minutes of ear closed seven rows

One gun (Tactix-3D4F), `$VOL,80`, every clip confirmed SOLO per F43's rule. Verdicts appended to
`~/.brx-mcp/sound-audit.jsonl` (104 rows across two sittings, 101 new ids, 249 distinct ids audited overall, `"via":"mcp-session"`). BLE dropped four times in ~90 minutes
of idle-with-`$PLAY` and reconnected clean each time, no data lost.

### A. `$PLAY`'s two slots are INTERRUPT and QUEUE, not "effect vs voice" (closes F42.1)

The manual and the protocol page both describe token 1 and token 4 as independent channels with no
stated mixing rule -- B6b on the bench queue asked exactly this. Six trials settle it: **token 1 is an
interrupt channel, token 4 is a FIFO queue, and it does not matter whether the id in a slot is an "fx"
id or a "voice" id.**

| # | frame(s) | result | reading |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | one frame, both slots: `$PLAY,H02,4,6,VA33,,,,*` | hammer, then "Game over", in sequence | a single frame does not layer the two slots |
| 2 | slot 4 (VA33), then slot 1 (H02) 300 ms later | "Gam.. HIT" -- the effect cut the voice off mid-word | slot 1 interrupts whatever slot 4 is doing |
| 3 | slot 1 (X20, 5 s artillery), then slot 4 (VA33) at 1 s | full artillery played out, then "game over" | slot 4 waits behind slot 1 rather than cutting it |
| 4 | slot 4 (VA33), then slot 4 (VA81) 300 ms later | game over, then the countdown, in order | two slot-4 ids queue behind each other |
| 5 | slot 1 (X20), then slot 1 (H02) at 1 s | the hammer interrupted the artillery | two slot-1 ids interrupt each other, same as #2 |
| 6 | slot 4 (VA33), then an fx id in **slot 4** (H02) at 300 ms | "game over then hammer, it waited" | an fx id dropped into slot 4 queues like a voice line -- the behaviour is the SLOT's, not the id's |
| 7 | three slot-4 lines 200 ms apart (VA33, VA81, VA7H) | all three played, in order, twice | queue depth is at least 3 |

So: **token 1 = INTERRUPT** (a new slot-1 id cuts whatever is playing, in either slot), **token 4 =
QUEUE** (a new slot-4 id waits its turn). Consequence for anything we build: a sound that must not step
on an announcer line ships in slot 4 regardless of whether it "feels like" an effect; anything
hit-path/urgent stays in slot 1 and accepts that it can cut a line off mid-word. `sounds --audit`'s
solo-confirm mode plays everything in slot 1, which is fine for a one-off listen but is not the shape a
real game uses for voice lines -- do not generalize a slot-1 audition to how an id behaves in slot 4.

This closes **F42.1**: `presentation.py`'s token-4 shape for `game_over` (VA33 in slot 4, alongside the
kill/medal/multi frames) is the one to keep. `compile.py`'s `Compiler.cues()` `game_over`/`medal`/`multi`
keys are unreachable -- `presentation.cue_frames` overwrites them for the same resolved profile and
nothing reads the stale copies (`state._push_voice_preview` reads only `kill`, which already agrees) --
so they are dead code, safe to delete on the next pass. VA81 in slot 1 for the countdown stays confirmed
and correct. Contract amendment **A21** (`docs/spec/contracts.md` §10, `presentation.events.<event>.slot`)
already writes this down as data -- a per-event `slot` field -- rather than a hardcoded token map.

### B. F59 (audio lags LED by ~1 s): the clips are RULED OUT, offline

New tool, `mcp/tools/soundbank_leadin.py` (stdlib + numpy, reads the raw s16le/44.1 kHz `.LTP` bank,
finds where each clip first crosses -40 dBFS relative to its own peak in 5 ms windows). Every shipped
hit id leads in immediately: H02/H36/H37/H22/H43/H06 all measure 0.000 s. Across all 2,477 ids on the
gun, only three exceed 0.5 s and none exceed 1.0 s, and the worst (J100 0.90 s, V108 0.90 s, SW31 0.57 s)
are long ambient/rules tracks nowhere near the hit path. Every id in `presentation.EVENTS` tops out
around 0.1-0.2 s (VB0E 0.195 s, VA7H 0.12 s).

That rules out lead-in silence as the cause of the ~1 s lag Tony heard on 2026-09-09. What is left is
firmware latency after `$PLAY` itself, which needs one filmed rung, not more theorizing: phone at 240
fps, a batched `$GLED` white + `$PLAY` frame, three numbers off the video -- a slot-4 line, a slot-1
effect, and a real hit. F59 stays open, narrowed to that one measurement (`eyes` + a camera).

### C. Ear verdicts that change shipped ids

- **F58(a) closed.** `healed` = the character's own slot-7 line (`voice:healed`; V37 "patched up",
  clean male take; V87 "Bleeding stopped", clean female take). `armour_up` = VA1G "Body Armor." (Halo-style
  announcer; VA16 "armor suit" also clean, not chosen). `shield_up` = VA8C "Shields online", with a sound
  effect. F58 stays open: (d), the `$LIFE` inconsistency, is untouched by tonight.
- **S9 mostly closed.** `extraction_closing` = V114, "10 seconds", Halo voice (VX0R failed -- it is the
  numbers/menu announcer, not the field announcer). `extraction_complete` = VS7, "Objective complete"

  as a note, not chosen). `unstoppable` = VX0U, "Domination", Halo voice. `killing_spree` stays VA7K
  (both VA7K and V125 read clean; VA7K keeps the sting and matches the VA7H/VA7E/VA7Q family already
  shipped). **`extraction_tick` = JAS, in the QUEUE slot (token 4), not U100.** Tony heard JAS (10.7 s)
  as "a cool extraction sound, intro hype music" and said the sound needs to loop for extraction -- the
  tick event already re-fires every ~10 s while the window is running, so JAS in slot 4 queues cleanly and
  never cuts an announcer line off (see the interrupt/queue finding above). JAQ (11 s, "good extraction
  sound too, it has ticking in the background") is the candidate for the window-open phase, not chosen

- **F45 closed.** All four never-heard `$PSET` tokens now have picks. `U15` confirmed "disabled trigger

- **W4a closed.** Energy Launcher fire = **O06** ("shooting a rocket"). O04 ("shooting a missile") is the

  ships with no sound at all today -- H23 hit then geiger counter, H24 metal bucket breaking, H25 plate
  breaking, H26 picking up / swiping something up, H27 bat, H28 clanking swords, H30/H31 shooting or
  launching, kind of squishy. The full transcript is in the audit file.
  **Second sitting, same night: the category is COMPLETE.** H32-H58 and the HM block heard solo: H34 "sword cutting something squishy", **H35/H39/H40/H54 "hammer hitting metal"** (four more armour-family takes, none adopted, A17's pool stays three), H41 breaking open then pieces scattering, H42 wooden clap, H44 "falling down, hitting the ground", H45 a metal chair falling over, H46 splash, H47 wooden hit then squish, H48 sword cut and squish, H51 laser shoot, **H52/H53/H58 "metal bucket"**, H55/H56 dart on a dart board, HM10/HM11/HM12 a small explosion in sand with pieces falling, HM13/HM14 small glass break, HM1B a cartoonish boing then a rapidly accelerating clock tick, HM1F equipping a big heavy weapon, HM1O/HM25 launcher or missile shots (launcher alternates beside O06). Totals: 252 rows / 249 distinct ids in the audit file (101 new tonight); `fx:hit` 122/122 (105 here + 17 judged 2026-09-07 in `hitaudio.py`).

### Method notes

The BLE link dropped four times in ~90 minutes of idle-with-`$PLAY`; each reconnect was clean, no data
lost. Tony's shorthand `a` in the audit tool means replay.

### D. The shield loop, live (F44 closed), and a lever nobody had pulled: `$LIFE` fills the shield pool

Same gun, later the same night. Armed Tactix-3D4F with the golden bundle's head over the MCP (33 frames, 100 ms
apart, `$LCD,45,70,0,0,32,192` back after `$SPAWN`), with the `$PSET` foot's last token (position 7,
`energyShieldLoop`) rewritten per trial. Then `$LIFE,0,0,20,*` -> `$HP,45,70,20` and the slot LOOPED;
`$LIFE,0,0,-20,*` -> `$HP,45,70,0` and it stopped, every time. Three trials, each heard alone:

| slot | Tony | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| U100 (the tick he had nominated earlier) | "kinda sounds like a bomb ticking about to blow" | rejected |
| U13 (the brighter tick) | "nah, I know I've heard a halo shield sound in game before" | rejected |
| **A10** (Callsign's own loop, rejected 2026-09-07) | "oh this is a nice humming of shield or energy" | **the shield loop** |

So the 2026-09-07 rejection of A10 was CONTEXT, not the clip: that night it ran under a barrage of shield-band
hits while the operator was trying to name hit sounds, and a loop under every hit is incoherent. Alone it is
the hum he wanted. A shape search could never have found this (F43 again): the answer was the id we already
had. Two neighbours, heard solo as one-shots: **A08** "kinda getting armor or protection on", **A09** "a sad
sound, maybe LOSING shield" -- which matches the app's `$FSET` enum order ShieldOnHeal / ShieldOffExpire /
ShieldLoop (`protocol-classes.md`), so A08/A09/A10 are Callsign's shield set. A08/A09 are recorded, not wired
(`shield_up` keeps VA8C; there is no shield_down event).

**The lever.** F60 says no compiled game can receive a heal or a shield, and the bench queue said F44 was
blocked on it. Both are IR statements: `$LIFE`'s third token fills the SHIELD pool over BLE on a table with no
proto-1 row at all (2026-09-09 proved `$LIFE` on hp/armour; tonight on shields, both directions). Tony: "you
figured out how to grant shield? i thought we couldnt do that". Filed as **F109**: overshield perks, BLE/phone
stations, timed regen and a LAN medic role are all node-written `$LIFE` frames, gated only by the unmeasured
per-grant cap (F58 (d)).

**S12 decided** the same hour: "let the config drive it. yeah silenced snipers no grunts could be legit" ->
`presentation.voice` = on / hits_only / off, `silenced` -> off, death scream untouched (A22, built tonight).
Gun left on the stock `$SIR` table (never on a bare `$CLEAR`).

## 2026-09-12 (desk, midday) — milestone 2: Block D built, the frag-cap end is replayed from the facts, phones report their build, a link proves the headset

No hardware. Seven build lanes on disjoint files against the spec rows written in the morning (A24–A32), a two-round
polish loop per side (read-only reviewers → fix lanes, every fix with a fail-first test), and a real-browser pass on
every changed screen. Two fix lanes were cut off by the account's session limit; their remaining items are F129.

**Built and verified:**
- **A24 results.** MC pushes `result` per recipient at the whistle (`win`/`lose`/`draw`/`undecided`, every row, honors,
  possession, the unofficial `after_end` block), re-sends it while provisional, carries it in `welcome.node.result`
  during recap; `score.rows` in every mode. The phone's FINAL RESULTS screen never infers an outcome: `RESULT PENDING ·
  CONFIRM AT MISSION CONTROL` until the push, `MC NOT REACHED` after 30 s; TEAMS|PLAYERS views, HISTORY, the cap−1
  off-range line on DOWN, the A31 `mc_verify` line on ARMED. **Trap found on the way:** `result` was in the phone's
  `MC_KINDS` but not in `transport.js DELIVERED`, so every push decoded and reached nothing — the F105 shape again, now
  pinned by a parity test over every `onMcMessage` case (engine AND utility.js).
- **The frag-cap end is RE-DERIVED (Tony's rule).** The match ends at the timestamp of the kill that reached the cap;
  a late-flushed fact with `t ≤ end_t + CLOCK_TIE_MS` (1 s) rebuilds the scorer from the stored facts (`kinds IN` SQL
  filter, ordered by `t`, deduped by node/seq) with the roster FROZEN at the whistle; the end can only move EARLIER (a
  late friendly kill in the band cannot move it forward); post-end facts ride the recap as "after the whistle", never
  counted; ties inside the band are `winner.tie`. A player re-teamed or added during the debrief is told the outcome of
  the side they wore, or nothing. Residual F130: a never-synced node's facts are scored at `t_recv` and cannot move the end.
- **A25 log sync.** `app/src/logsync.js` answers `pull_log` only when not armed/live and the fact ring is empty, else
  parks it (5→60 s backoff), uploads one chunk at a time behind `bufferedAmount`, sends only the tail on a later pull, and
  halves a refused chunk; MC asks at recap / on offer / on a reconnect that still owes the last match's log / from the
  ⬇ LOGS button, gated by `log_sync` (`GET/PUT /api/options`), one outstanding ask per node, a per-match budget;
  `NodeView.log` states on the readiness board. `npm run ui:logsync` is a 17-check browser gate against the real net server.
- **A29 build report.** `npm run build` bakes `<package.json version>+<sha>[-dirty]` (the app had sent a hard-coded
  `hud-0.2`); `hello` and every `status` carry `app_ver` + `platform`. MC parses semver, holds `APP_MAJOR/APP_MINOR`
  (0.x compares `(major, minor)`), flags red on an incompatible build and amber behind the field or the published
  release, shows a per-phone chip and a muster tally. A test pins the constants to app/package.json so a bump on one side
  fails at the desk.
- **A32 headset by link.** `headset: proven` after `gun_linked` is true for 10 s (`HEADSET_LINK_PROOF_MS`), `CONFIRMING
  (LINK n s)` amber meanwhile, un-proved by a drop (echo proofs too), never aged into a proof for an offline phone.
- **A26 try-out collapse.** A weapon row tap equips AND arms after a 400 ms debounce, ⟳ then ✓, `REVIEW KIT ▸`, ⓘ per row.
  **A30 / A27.** The kit locks at START on both sides (phone picks refused with a reason, host edits 409, a node that took
  this match's config is never re-headed, late joiners still hot-join); `POST /api/phase` 409 unless `force` after a named
  warning; the phone says `THE HOST LOCKED KITS`. **A real defect from the doc-rot pass, confirmed and fixed:** `resume()`
  in LIVE ran the retired trigger-first resync; it now reconciles like the BLE-reconnect path.
- **S24/S25.** Live board legibility (11 px headers, grouped numerics from one column model, `best_streak`, `~` settling
  mark, `<Num>` fixed-width digits — no product font has tabular figures), a repo-wide sweep to ≥ 11 px, `#spectate`
  read-only projector route (no command bar, rows fit the viewport, dims when frozen, result card in recap).
- **S22 sidearms** (see FOLLOWUPS archive) and **D5 CAM dropped**.

**Verified:** `python3 mcp/run_tests.py` 1403+/0 under a 4 GB cap · app 333/333 · MC UI 247/247, lint + build clean ·
`e2e:kit`, `e2e:m2` (measure/mock/phone/real/refusal/stale/offline), `e2e` koth, `ui:logsync` all green · HUD screen-truth
